<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Under the characteristic hypotheses of restricted geometric Langlands theory, we prove that the canonical Arthur filtration on finitely supported unramified automorphic functions is defined over ℚ for split connected semisimple groups. The result includes the noncuspidal part and every closed invariant nilpotent support, establishing the rationality conjecture of Gaitsgory–Lafforgue–Raskin in this setting.

- **claim-rational-arthur-filtration** (theorem, [source](#stmt-intro-main)): Under Assumption <a href="#intro:characteristic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="intro:characteristic">1</a>, for every prime $\ell\ne\operatorname{char}(\mathbb F_q)$ and every closed invariant $Y\subset\widehat{\mathcal N}$, the natural map $$\label{intro:main-map} E\otimes_\mathbb Q\bigl(\mathcal A_\mathbb Q\cap\mathcal F_Y\mathcal A_E\bigr) \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathcal F_Y\mathcal A_E$$ is an isomorphism. The intersection is taken inside $\mathcal A_E$.
  - Explanation: The canonical Arthur filtration descends to rational finitely supported automorphic functions, including the noncuspidal part.
- **claim-restricted-langlands-equivalence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-inp-langlands)): There is a Frobenius-invariant union $Z$ of connected components of $\mathop{\mathrm{LocSys}}^{\mathrm{restr}}_{\widehat G}(\overline X)$ and an equivalence $$\mathbb L_G^{\mathrm{restr}}:\mathcal C \xrightarrow{\sim}\mathop{\mathrm{IndCoh}}_{\widehat{\mathcal N}}(Z).$$ It identifies $\mathcal C_Y$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{IndCoh}}_Y(Z)$ for every closed $\widehat G$-invariant $Y\subset\widehat{\mathcal N}$ and intertwines Hecke functors with tensoring by the corresponding evaluation bundles. If $f:Z\to Z$ sends a local system to its pullback along geometric Frobenius of $\overline X$, the functor corresponding to $\Phi=(\operatorname{Frob}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Bun}}})_*$ is $f^!$. The embedding $\mathcal C\hookrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}(\mathop{\mathrm{Bun}}_G(\overline X),\overline{\mathbb Q}_{\ell})$ preserves compact objects.
  - Explanation: The retained spectral union gives the automorphic equivalence, preserving support bounds, evaluation actions, Frobenius conventions and compact objects.
- **claim-trace-ordinary-local-terms** (theorem, [source](#stmt-inp-trace)): There is a canonical isomorphism of complexes $$\operatorname{LT}:\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\Phi,\mathcal C)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal A_{\overline{\mathbb Q}_{\ell}},$$ with the right-hand side in degree zero. For a compact object $M$ and a morphism $b:M\to\Phi M$, it sends the categorical class $[M,b]$ to the ordinary alternating trace function of the adjoint morphism $(\operatorname{Frob}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Bun}}})^*M\to M$. The function has finite support. This comparison is compatible with Hecke operations and with the sheaf–function operations of pullback, star tensor product, and compactly supported pushforward.
  - Explanation: The ambient categorical trace is the full space of finitely supported automorphic functions, and compact trace classes have their ordinary sheaf trace functions.
- **claim-nonstandard-perfect-duality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-inp-duality)): The pairing $$\label{inp:duality-pairing} \mathcal C\otimes\mathcal C\longrightarrow\operatorname{Vect}_{\overline{\mathbb Q}_{\ell}},\qquad (M,N)\longmapsto R\Gamma_c\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{Bun}}_G(\overline X),M\overset{*}{\otimes}N\bigr)$$ defines a self-duality of $\mathcal C$. It is invariant under simultaneous Frobenius. If $M,N$ are compact, its value is a perfect complex, also after any finite Hecke representation is inserted in either variable.
  - Explanation: Compactly supported star tensor product defines a Frobenius invariant self-duality whose compact evaluations remain perfect after finite Hecke insertions.
- **claim-component-formal-geometry** (theorem, [source](#stmt-inp-components)): Every connected component $Z_\sigma$ of the restricted local-system stack has a unique semisimple isomorphism class $\sigma$. Its automorphism group $J=\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(\sigma)$ is reductive, possibly disconnected. There is a formal affine coarse space $S_\sigma$ with reduced space a point, and $Z_\sigma\to S_\sigma$ is relatively algebraic. After choosing a frame at a geometric point, the component is formal affine. For an affine infinitesimal stage $S_n\to S_\sigma$, its base change is affine; almost finite type stages and bounded Postnikov truncations may be used. These affine stages have coherent cohomology in each bounded range. The tangent complex at a local system $\tau$ is $$\label{inp:tangent} T_\tau\mathop{\mathrm{LocSys}}^{\mathrm{restr}}_{\widehat G}(\overline X) \simeq R\Gamma(\overline X,\widehat{\mathfrak g}_\tau)[1].$$ In particular the stack is quasi-smooth.
  - Explanation: Each restricted component has a unique semisimple point with reductive stabilizer, coherent formal affine stages, and the adjoint cohomology tangent complex.
- **claim-pure-weil-lift** (theorem, [source](#stmt-inp-pure-lift)): Fix an abstract field isomorphism $\iota:\overline{\mathbb Q}_{\ell}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb C$. If $Z_\sigma$ is Frobenius invariant, its semisimple point $\sigma$ admits a Weil structure that is $\iota$-pure of weight zero. In particular, writing $\widehat{\mathfrak g}_\sigma$ for its adjoint local system, every Frobenius eigenvalue on $H^i(\overline X,\widehat{\mathfrak g}_\sigma)$ has $\iota$-absolute value $q^{i/2}$, for $0\leq i\leq2$.
  - Explanation: A fixed semisimple parameter has a pure Weil lift, giving exact absolute Frobenius weights on its adjoint cohomology without asserting diagonalizability.
- **claim-finite-covariant-recovery** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mod-finite-covariants)): Let $K$ be a reductive complex algebraic group, possibly disconnected, and let $A$ be a finitely generated $K$-algebra. Suppose $A^K$ is Artinian and $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}A$ has a unique closed $K$-orbit $O$, with ideal $I$. For an equivariant finite $A$-module $M$ and a finite-dimensional $K$-representation $D$, the inverse system $$\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_K(D,M/I^nM)$$ is eventually constant with value $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_K(D,M)$. In particular the rational, locally $K$-finite part of the $I$-adic completion recovers $M$, and recovers $A$ together with its multiplication when $M=A$.
  - Explanation: Finite representation tests stabilize on orbit jets and recover rational equivariant modules and their algebra structures from completion.
- **claim-invariant-base-reconstruction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mod-reconstruction)): For the framed component above, an equivariant isomorphism of its completion along $K/J$ with another such completed model extends uniquely over the formal invariant bases to an isomorphism of their invariant-fiber completions. The same assertion holds for equivariant maps and their full mapping spaces, and for finite equivariant presentation data. Here the extension is unique up to a contractible space of choices.
  - Explanation: An equivariant identification constructed at the closed orbit recovers the invariant-fiber completion, including maps, homotopies and finite presentation data.
- **claim-jordan-nonresonance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mod-nonresonance)): Let a minimal complete free graded-commutative algebra have generators $V^*$ in cohomological degree $0$ and $R^*$ in degree $-1$. Suppose a continuous graded automorphism has linear parts of absolute weights $-1$ and $-2$ on these two generator spaces. It is conjugate, by a formal graded coordinate change tangent to the identity, to its linear part. If it commutes with a minimal differential, that differential, in the resulting coordinates, sends $R^*$ to quadratic polynomials in $V^*$. These assertions hold equivariantly for a reductive group normalized by the automorphism.
  - Explanation: Separated absolute generator weights formally linearize the automorphism and force a commuting minimal differential to retain only quadratic relations, including with Jordan blocks.
- **claim-framed-quadratic-model** (construction, [source](#stmt-mod-quadratic-model)): The retained component $Z_\sigma$, with its Frobenius action, evaluation bundles at the chosen cyclic tuple, and singular-support map, admits the model $$\label{mod:quadratic-equation} Z_\sigma\simeq \bigl[(\mu^{-1}(0))^{\wedge}_{\mathrm{unst}}/J\bigr].$$ Here $\mathrm{unst}$ is the inverse image of the origin under $V\to V/\!/J$, and completion is along this invariant null fiber. In this model:* 1. *the singularity scheme has points $(x,z)$ satisfying $\mu(x)=0$ and $zx=0$, with $z\in\mathfrak j$;* 2. *the support condition defined by $Y$ is the condition that the image of $z$ under $\mathfrak j\hookrightarrow\widehat{\mathfrak g}$ belongs to $Y$;* 3. *every evaluation representation of $K=\widehat G^d$ is the bundle associated to its restriction along $J\hookrightarrow K$; Frobenius has constant cyclic transport on these bundles;* 4. *after removing the scalars $q^{1/2}$ on deformation points and $q^{-1}$ on singular points, Frobenius is a linear automorphism of the moment-map data, normalizing $J$ and the framed representation labels. Its eigenvalues on these normalized linear data have modulus one.* *The meaning of Frobenius on Hom complexes in <span class="upright">(iv)</span> is fixed explicitly in Lemma <a href="#mod:hom-action" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mod:hom-action">13</a> below.
  - Explanation: Each retained fixed component has a derived moment-map presentation completed along the invariant null fiber, retaining evaluation bundles and the singular-support map.
- **claim-inverse-hom-transport** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mod-hom-action)): Choose structures $\alpha_M:\Phi M\xrightarrow{\sim}M$ on compact objects. On an arrow $h:M\to N$ put $$\begin{aligned} T(h)&=\alpha_N\,\Phi(h)\,\alpha_M^{-1},\label{mod:T}\\ S(h)&=\Phi^{-1}(\alpha_N^{-1}h\alpha_M)=T^{-1}(h). \label{mod:S} \end{aligned}$$ The action in the bilinear Hom pairing is $S$. On the quadratic model it gives absolute weight $-1$ to deformation functions and absolute weight $+2$ to degree-two obstruction operators. Thus its associated point weights on $(x,z)$ are $(1,-2)$, and $W(x,z)=\langle z,\mu(x)\rangle$ has weight zero.* *The graph identification in <a href="#tr:graph-identification" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="tr:graph-identification">[tr:graph-identification]</a> turns the original $\Phi$-graph bimodule into the graph of the transported arrow action. In those coordinates the induced simultaneous bar actions $T$ and $S$ are inverse. If the paracyclic homotopy is $bH+Hb=1-T$, then $$\label{mod:inverse-homotopy} b(-T^{-1}H)+(-T^{-1}H)b=1-S.$$ In particular using contracting $S$-weights does not replace $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\Phi,-)$ by $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\Phi^{-1},-)$.
  - Explanation: The bilinear Hom action is inverse transport. Its contracting weights and transported bar homotopy compute the original categorical Frobenius trace.
- **claim-simultaneous-compact-normalization** (construction, [source](#stmt-mod-compact-normalization)): Let $J.e$ be a nilpotent orbit preserved by the normalized transporter in Theorem <a href="#mod:quadratic-model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mod:quadratic-model">12</a>. After changing that transporter by an element of $J$, choose a triple $(e,h,f)$ in this orbit and write the normalized transporter as $$u=t\,v_0.$$ There are simultaneous compact forms of the group and frame data such that:* 1. *$t$ belongs to a compact group, preserves the compact form $J_{\mathrm{cpt}}$, and centralizes the triple;* 2. *$v_0$ is unipotent, centralizes $J$, and commutes with $t$;* 3. *for $L_e=Z_J(\mathrm{SL}_{2,e})$, $L_{e,\mathrm{cpt}}=L_e\cap J_{\mathrm{cpt}}$ is a maximal compact including its components, and every $k t v_0$, $k\in L_{e,\mathrm{cpt}}$, has modulus-one eigenvalues on all the normalized linear data.* *These assertions also hold on their tensor, symmetric, exterior, subquotient, and triple-weight constructions. After the orbit-fixing translation by $\lambda_e(\sqrt q)$, the semisimple value of the frame transport around the $d$-cycle belongs to $$\label{mod:cyclic-compact-value} \bigl\{[\lambda_e(q^{d/2})s]: s\in Z_{\widehat G}(\mathrm{SL}_{2,e})_{\mathrm{cpt}}\bigr\} =B_{\widehat G.e}.$$ This holds for every $k\in L_{e,\mathrm{cpt}}$.
  - Explanation: The normalized transporter has compatible compact and commuting unipotent factors, uniformly controlling compact stabilizer twists and cyclic frame values.
- **claim-koszul-support-equivalence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-orb-koszul)): The compact ind-coherent category on the uncompleted derived quotient $[\mu^{-1}(0)/J]$ is the category of finite coherent graded $J$-equivariant factorizations of <a href="#orb:potential" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="orb:potential">[orb:potential]</a>. Coherent singular support in a closed cone $\Omega\subset\mathfrak j$ corresponds to local vanishing of the factorization off $V\times\Omega$. The completion in Theorem <a href="#mod:quadratic-model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mod:quadratic-model">12</a> additionally imposes ordinary support on the inverse image of $0\in\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}R^J$.
  - Explanation: Graded factorizations describe compact objects on the derived quotient. Singular-direction support and ordinary invariant null-fiber support remain distinct conditions.
- **claim-supported-localization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-orb-localization)): Let $\Omega'\subset\Omega$ be closed invariant cones. Restriction to the complementary open gives the compactly generated localization with compact quotient $\mathscr B_\Omega/\mathscr B_{\Omega'}$. If $\mathcal O$ is an orbit closed in that open, its supported compact category is generated by coherent curved pushforwards from $V\times\mathcal O$. Morphisms are computed in the open before taking derived equivariant sections. The statements also hold with invariant null-fiber support.
  - Explanation: Nested support cones give compactly generated localization, and a closed orbit in the complementary open is generated by coherent curved pushforwards.
- **claim-semisimple-orbit-generation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-orb-generation)): The objects $M_P$, for simple $P\in\mathsf E_e$, generate the compact category supported on $J.e$ in an open where it is closed. Here “generate” allows finite cones, shifts and summands. The category $\mathsf E_e$ is semisimple.
  - Explanation: Simple equivariant Clifford labels generate the orbit-supported compact category through finite triangulated operations, and their coefficient category is semisimple.
- **claim-compact-orbit-closure-weil-lifts** (construction, [source](#stmt-orb-weil-lifts)): Every $M_P$ is the restriction of a compact object with $z$-support in $\overline{J.e}$. If $P$ has a compatible locally finite cyclic structure, the extension can be given that structure. For a simple label fixed by Frobenius, a structure may be chosen whose triple-translated coefficient eigenvalues have modulus one. The same assertions hold for a power of Frobenius, for a finite cyclic list of labels, and after tensoring $P$ by any finite-dimensional $L_e$-representation with compatible cyclic structure, placed in lifted degree zero. Inverse structures are available for opposite category classes.
  - Explanation: Orbit generators extend as actual compact objects on their closures while retaining compatible cyclic structures, pure fixed-label normalizations and opposite-category structures.
- **claim-orbit-hom-euler-and-weights** (theorem, [source](#stmt-orb-hom-calculation)): For $P,P'\in\mathsf E_e$, the Hom complex between the orbit generators has a finite equivariant calculation with Euler terms $$\label{orb:hom-euler} \left[ \mathbb C[\ker(e:V\to V)]\otimes \mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{D_e}(P,P')\otimes \Lambda^\bullet(N_e[-1])\otimes \Lambda^\bullet(\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(U_e)^*[-1]) \right]^{L_e}.$$ Here the coefficient factor has lifted degree zero; the shifts in the normal and Lie factors add one to the degrees in <a href="#orb:weight-table" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="orb:weight-table">[orb:weight-table]</a>. The formula is an equality of alternating equivariant characters, and a finite-filtration calculation bounding weights, rather than an assertion that the Hom complex splits as the displayed tensor product.* *For pure coefficient normalizations, all nonconstant terms have strictly negative absolute $S_e$-weight. The weight-zero cohomology is $$\label{orb:weight-zero-hom} H^\bullet\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(M_P,M_{P'})_{\mathrm{wt}=0} =\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{D_e,L_e}(P,P')[0],$$ with ordinary composition. Before the finite invariant calculation, weight pieces are finite-dimensional and their dimensions grow at most polynomially over bounded-length weight intervals. In particular, the character sums in <a href="#orb:hom-euler" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="orb:hom-euler">[orb:hom-euler]</a> are absolutely convergent on every normalized compact $L_e$-coset, uniformly on that coset.
  - Explanation: A finite equivariant filtration computes the alternating Hom character and bounds its weights. Only constant equivariant Clifford intertwiners survive at weight zero.
- **claim-invariant-support-trace-isomorphism** (theorem, [source](#stmt-tr-invariant-support)): For every Frobenius-stable conical $z$-support bound $\Omega$, inclusion induces an isomorphism $$\label{tr:remove-completion} \mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\Phi,\operatorname{Ind}\mathscr B_{\Omega,0}) \xrightarrow{\;\sim\;} \mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\Phi,\operatorname{Ind}\mathscr B_\Omega).$$ A compact Weil object on the right has a compact Weil lift on the left whose trace class maps to a nonzero scalar multiple of its class. Both statements hold for the compact opposite categories. Moreover, for Hom pairings of two such lifts, the finite Koszul construction multiplies each orbit contribution by a constant nonzero factor, independent of the compact stabilizer variable.
  - Explanation: Invariant null-fiber support does not change the trace. Finite Koszul lifts preserve actual compact Weil representatives up to nonzero scalar factors, including orbitwise Hom contributions.
- **claim-locally-finite-bar-contraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-tr-bar-contraction)): Let a small generating differential graded category with an autoequivalence be modeled in complexes of locally finite cyclic representations. Suppose, after finite cyclic permutations of its labels and pure choices of their structures, its morphism cohomology has nonpositive absolute $S$-weights; composition adds weights. Suppose its weight-zero morphism cohomology is concentrated in degree zero and its additive idempotent completion is semisimple, with endomorphism algebra $\mathbb C$ for every simple object. Then its twisted trace is the twisted trace of that split semisimple category. It is concentrated in degree zero, with one basis line for each fixed simple label. Nontrivially permuted simple labels contribute zero.
  - Explanation: Algebraic contraction removes strictly negative-weight bar terms, leaving the trace of the split semisimple weight-zero category and only fixed simple labels.
- **claim-orbit-trace-basis** (theorem, [source](#stmt-tr-orbit-trace)): The trace on the supported category of a Frobenius-stable nilpotent orbit is concentrated in degree zero. A basis is given by the classes of $M_P$ for Frobenius-fixed simple $P\in\mathsf E_e$, with chosen pure normalized structures. Every basis class lifts to an actual compact Weil object supported on the orbit closure, and also to one with invariant null-fiber support up to a nonzero scalar. The same conclusions hold for the compact opposite category with simultaneous Frobenius.
  - Explanation: A stable orbit has a degree-zero trace basis indexed by fixed simple Clifford labels, with actual compact closure lifts and invariant null-fiber lifts.
- **claim-supported-trace-injection-and-spanning** (theorem, [source](#stmt-tr-spanning)): On a fixed retained spectral component, the trace of every closed invariant nilpotent support category is concentrated in degree zero. If $\Omega'\subset\Omega$ are such support bounds, then $$\label{tr:support-injection} H^0\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\Phi,\operatorname{Ind}\mathscr B_{\Omega',0}) \longrightarrow H^0\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\Phi,\operatorname{Ind}\mathscr B_{\Omega,0})$$ is injective. A finite filtration by invariant unions of orbits has the orbit traces as its actual successive quotients. Every trace class is a finite linear combination of classes of compact objects with Weil structures; those objects can be chosen with support in the prescribed bound. All assertions hold on the compact opposite side.* *After assembling retained components, these statements apply to $\mathcal C_Y$ and $\mathcal C$. In particular, the image defining $\mathcal F_Y\mathcal A_{\overline{\mathbb Q}_{\ell}}$ is the injective image of the supported trace, and every function in $\mathcal A_{\overline{\mathbb Q}_{\ell}}$ and every vector in $\mathcal F_Y\mathcal A_{\overline{\mathbb Q}_{\ell}}$ is a finite linear combination of the corresponding actual compact Weil trace functions. The analogous statement supplies all tests through the nonstandard self-duality of Theorem <a href="#inp:duality" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="inp:duality">6</a>.
  - Explanation: Finite orbit localization assembles concentrated traces with injective support inclusions and actual compact Weil spanning on both sides of the automorphic duality.
- **claim-bilinear-support-detector** (theorem, [source](#stmt-meas-detector)): For every $f,g\in\mathcal A_{\mathbb C}$ there is a unique finite complex Borel measure $\nu_{f,g}$ on $B$ such that, for every finite-dimensional representation $D$ of $\widehat G$, $$\label{meas:moments} [T_Df,g]=\int_B\chi_D\,d\nu_{f,g}.$$ For every closed invariant subset $Y\subset\widehat{\mathcal N}$, $$\label{meas:criterion} f\in\mathcal F_Y\mathcal A_{\mathbb C} \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad \operatorname{supp}(\nu_{f,g})\subset B_Y \quad\text{for every }g\in\mathcal A_{\mathbb C}.$$
  - Explanation: Hecke matrix coefficients determine unique finite complex measures, and support of all bilinear test measures exactly characterizes each Arthur filtration subspace.
- **claim-hom-function-supertrace** (theorem, [source](#stmt-meas-hom-functions)): Let $N$ and $M$ be compact spectral objects with the structures just specified, and let $f_N$ and $g_M$ be their automorphic trace functions, where $M$ is transported through the categorical duality. Then $$\label{meas:hom-function-formula} [T_Df_N,g_M] =\operatorname{str}\!\left( S_{M,N,D};\operatorname{RHom}(M,\mathcal E_{D,v}\otimes N)\right).$$ Here $\mathcal E_{D,v}$ denotes the evaluation insertion at the cyclic tuple above $v$. The scalar evaluation complex is perfect. Finite linear combinations of these classes give all inputs and all tests in $\mathcal A_{\mathbb C}$. Inputs in $\mathcal F_Y\mathcal A_{\mathbb C}$ may be represented by such $N$ with the prescribed $Y$-support.
  - Explanation: Compact spectral Hom evaluations compute ordinary Hecke pairings as perfect-complex supertraces, and their finite linear combinations supply all inputs and tests.
- **claim-supported-character-convergence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-meas-supported-convergence)): Let $O=J.e$ be a stable nilpotent orbit, closed in an invariant open subset of $\mathfrak j$. The contribution with support on $O$ to the Hom calculation has an absolutely convergent alternating character on the compact transporter coset of $L_e$. Convergence is uniform on that coset, also after any fixed finite-dimensional evaluation insertion. These characters are additive through the finite orbit filtration and compute the ordinary supertrace of the final perfect evaluation complex.
  - Explanation: Local cohomology along an orbit yields uniformly absolutely convergent alternating characters, additive through the orbit filtration and equal to the final ordinary supertrace.
- **claim-common-support-measure-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-meas-common-support)): Suppose $M,N$ belong to one retained component and have closed $z$-support bounds $Z_M,Z_N\subset\mathfrak j$. Their measure satisfies $$\label{meas:common-support-bound} \operatorname{supp}(\nu_{f_N,g_M}) \subset \bigcup_{e\in Z_M\cap Z_N}B_{\widehat G.e}.$$
  - Explanation: An input-test measure is supported only on compact spectral sets indexed by nilpotent directions in their common support.
- **claim-universal-density-cancellation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-meas-cancel-density)): The function $p_e$ is continuous and nowhere zero on $L_{e,c}$. Its reciprocal is a uniform limit of finite complex linear combinations of twisted characters of finite-dimensional $L_e$-representations with compatible transporter structures. Such a twisted character can be inserted in <a href="#meas:top-density" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="meas:top-density">[meas:top-density]</a> by tensoring the test coefficient with the dual representation, in lifted degree zero.
  - Explanation: The universal orbit density is invertible, and finite character approximants to its reciprocal are realized by allowed tensor twists of compact tests.
- **claim-top-quotient-nondegeneracy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-meas-top-detection)): Every nonzero finite linear combination of fixed-simple classes in the $O$-quotient is detected by a compact test supported on $\overline O$: its top-orbit measure has nonzero total mass on $B_{\widehat G.e}$.
  - Explanation: A nonzero finite fixed-simple quotient class admits an actual compact closure-supported test whose top-orbit measure has nonzero total mass.
- **claim-boundary-ambient-rank-drop** (bound, [source](#stmt-meas-boundary-rank)): If $e'\in\overline{J.e}\setminus J.e$, then $\dim(\widehat G.e')<\dim(\widehat G.e)$. In particular the two ambient nilpotent orbits, and their compact sets, are distinct.
  - Explanation: Every proper boundary orbit has strictly smaller ambient adjoint dimension, so its compact spectral set cannot cancel a top-orbit contribution.
- **claim-compact-spectral-set-separation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-desc-compactsets)): There are finitely many sets $B_o$. Each is compact and independent of the triple and compact form used to define it, and distinct orbits give disjoint sets. The algebra of representation characters restricted to $B=\bigcup_oB_o$ contains the constants, separates points and is closed under complex conjugation. It is uniformly dense in $C(B,\mathbb C)$. Each $B_o$ is preserved by inversion and by multiplication by a finite-order central element of $\widehat G_\mathbb C$.
  - Explanation: Nilpotent labels give disjoint choice-independent compact spectral sets. Representation characters are dense on their union, and inversion and finite central translation preserve each set.
- **claim-character-moment-uniqueness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-desc-unique-measure)): A finite complex Borel measure on $B$ is determined by its integrals against all representation characters.
  - Explanation: Representation-character moments uniquely determine finite complex Borel measures on the compact spectral union.
- **claim-coefficient-isomorphism-independence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-desc-coefficient-independence)): For a fixed closed invariant support $Y$, the subspace $\iota_*(\mathcal F_Y\mathcal A_E)\subset\mathcal A_\mathbb C$ is independent of the abstract isomorphism $\iota:E\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb C$, after matching nilpotent orbits by the split root datum. Consequently $\mathcal F_Y\mathcal A_E$ is invariant under every automorphism of $E$ fixing $\mathbb Q$.
  - Explanation: Matching orbit labels through the pinned split root datum makes the transported filtration independent of the abstract coefficient isomorphism and invariant under rational coefficient automorphisms.
- **claim-finite-coordinate-rational-descent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-desc-descent)): Let $E$ be an algebraically closed extension of $\mathbb Q$, and let $V_\mathbb Q$ have a specified basis, possibly infinite. If $W\subset E\otimes_\mathbb QV_\mathbb Q$ is invariant under every $\tau\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(E/\mathbb Q)$, then $$E\otimes_\mathbb Q(W\cap V_\mathbb Q)\longrightarrow W$$ is an isomorphism.
  - Explanation: Full coefficient-automorphism invariance forces rational reduced row-echelon bases on finite coordinate sets; their directed union descends the entire subspace.
- **claim-unramified-cuspidal-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-desc-cuspidal-comparison)): Keep the curve $X/\mathbb F_q$, the split connected semisimple group $G/\mathbb F_q$, and all four conditions of Assumption <a href="#intro:characteristic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="intro:characteristic">1</a>. Put $p=\operatorname{char}(\mathbb F_q)$ and $E_\ell=\overline{\mathbb Q}_\ell$ for $\ell\ne p$. At unramified level $D=0$, let $C_{0,\mathbb Q}\subset\mathcal A_\mathbb Q$ be the cuspidal function space and $C_{0,\mathcal O,\mathbb Q}$ its rational orbit summands from [[CuspidalArthur](#ref-CuspidalArthur), Theorem 1.1], and set $C_{0,\ell}=E_\ell\otimes_\mathbb QC_{0,\mathbb Q}$. For each $\ell\ne p$, let $Y_\ell\subset\widehat{\mathcal N}_{E_\ell}$ be closed and invariant, with the same nilpotent-orbit subset $\mathscr Y$ under the fixed pinned split rational dual group. Write $Y=(Y_\ell)_{\ell\ne p}$ and define $$F^{\mathbb Q}_{Y,\ell}:=\mathcal A_\mathbb Q\cap\mathcal F_{Y_\ell}\mathcal A_{E_\ell}, \qquad V_{Y,\mathbb Q}:=\bigoplus_{\mathcal O\in\mathscr Y}C_{0,\mathcal O,\mathbb Q}.$$ Then $$\label{desc:cuspidal-equalities} \begin{split} C_{0,\mathbb Q}\cap F^{\mathbb Q}_{Y,\ell}&=V_{Y,\mathbb Q},\\ C_{0,\ell}\cap\mathcal F_{Y_\ell}\mathcal A_{E_\ell} &=E_\ell\otimes_\mathbb QV_{Y,\mathbb Q}. \end{split}$$ The intersections are taken in the corresponding finitely supported function spaces. The equalities include the zero cusp space and empty or full $Y_\ell$.
  - Explanation: On the unramified cuspidal subspace, matched Arthur filtration steps are precisely the scalar extensions of the corresponding rational orbit summands across coefficient primes.

          "exact_quote": "There is a canonical isomorphism of complexes $$\\operatorname{LT}:\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}(\\Phi,\\mathcal C)\\xrightarrow{\\sim}\\mathcal A_{\\overline{\\mathbb Q}_{\\ell}},$$ with the right-hand side in degree zero. For a compact object $M$ and a morphism $b:M\\to\\Phi M$, it sends the categorical class $[M,b]$ to the ordinary alternating trace function of the adjoint morphism $(\\operatorname{Frob}_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Bun}}})^*M\\to M$. The function has finite support. This comparison is compatible with Hecke operations and with the sheaf–function operations of pullback, star tensor product, and compactly supported pushforward.",
          "source_id": "PM-014.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There is a canonical isomorphism of complexes $$\\operatorname{LT}:\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}(\\Phi,\\mathcal C)\\xrightarrow{\\sim}\\mathcal A_{\\overline{\\mathbb Q}_{\\ell}},$$ with the right-hand side in degree zero.       "explanation": "The ambient categorical trace is the full space of finitely supported automorphic functions, and compact trace classes have their ordinary sheaf trace functions.",

          "anchor": "stmt-intro-characteristic",
          "exact_quote": "The following four conditions hold.\n\n1.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-inp-components",
        "exact_quote": "Every connected component $Z_\\sigma$ of the restricted local-system stack has a unique semisimple isomorphism class $\\sigma$. Its automorphism group $J=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Aut}}(\\sigma)$ is reductive, possibly disconnected. There is a formal affine coarse space $S_\\sigma$ with reduced space a point, and $Z_\\sigma\\to S_\\sigma$ is relatively algebraic. After choosing a frame at a geometric point, the component is formal affine. For an affine infinitesimal stage $S_n\\to S_\\sigma$, its base change is affine; almost finite type stages and bounded Postnikov truncations may be used. These affine stages have coherent cohomology in each bounded range. The tangent complex at a local system $\\tau$ is $$\\label{inp:tangent}\n T_\\tau\\mathop{\\mathrm{LocSys}}^{\\mathrm{restr}}_{\\widehat G}(\\overline X)\n       \\simeq R\\Gamma(\\overline X,\\widehat{\\mathfrak g}_\\tau)[1].$$ In particular the stack is quasi-smooth.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-inp-pure-lift",
        "exact_quote": "Fix an abstract field isomorphism $\\iota:\\overline{\\mathbb Q}_{\\ell}\\xrightarrow{\\sim}\\mathbb C$. If $Z_\\sigma$ is Frobenius invariant, its semisimple point $\\sigma$ admits a Weil structure that is $\\iota$-pure of weight zero. In particular, writing $\\widehat{\\mathfrak g}_\\sigma$ for its adjoint local system, every Frobenius eigenvalue on $H^i(\\overline X,\\widehat{\\mathfrak g}_\\sigma)$ has $\\iota$-absolute value $q^{i/2}$, for $0\\leq i\\leq2$.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-inp-pure-lift",

          "source_id": "PM-014.2"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-mod-finite-covariants",
        "exact_quote": "Let $K$ be a reductive complex algebraic group, possibly disconnected, and let $A$ be a finitely generated $K$-algebra. Suppose $A^K$ is Artinian and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}A$ has a unique closed $K$-orbit $O$, with ideal $I$. For an equivariant finite $A$-module $M$ and a finite-dimensional $K$-representation $D$, the inverse system $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_K(D,M/I^nM)$$ is eventually constant with value $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_K(D,M)$. In particular the rational, locally $K$-finite part of the $I$-adic completion recovers $M$, and recovers $A$ together with its multiplication when $M=A$.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-mod-finite-covariants",
          "exact_quote": "Let $K$ be a reductive complex algebraic group, possibly disconnected, and let $A$ be a finitely generated $K$-algebra. In particular the rational, locally $K$-finite part of the $I$-adic completion recovers $M$, and recovers $A$ together with its multiplication when $M=A$.",

        }

        "anchor": "stmt-mod-reconstruction",
        "exact_quote": "For the framed component above, an equivariant isomorphism of its completion along $K/J$ with another such completed model extends uniquely over the formal invariant bases to an isomorphism of their invariant-fiber completions.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-mod-nonresonance",
        "exact_quote": "Let a minimal complete free graded-commutative algebra have generators $V^*$ in cohomological degree $0$ and $R^*$ in degree $-1$. Suppose a continuous graded automorphism has linear parts of absolute weights $-1$ and $-2$ on these two generator spaces. It is conjugate, by a formal graded coordinate change tangent to the identity, to its linear part. If it commutes with a minimal differential, that differential, in the resulting coordinates, sends $R^*$ to quadratic polynomials in $V^*$. These assertions hold equivariantly for a reductive group normalized by the automorphism.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-mod-quadratic-model",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"mod:model\" label=\"mod:model\"></span> The retained component $Z_\\sigma$, with its Frobenius action, evaluation bundles at the chosen cyclic tuple, and singular-support map, admits the model $$\\label{mod:quadratic-equation}\n Z_\\sigma\\simeq\n \\bigl[(\\mu^{-1}(0))^{\\wedge}_{\\mathrm{unst}}/J\\bigr].$$ Here $\\mathrm{unst}$ is the inverse image of the origin under $V\\to V/\\!/J$, and completion is along this invariant null fiber. In this model:*\n\n1.  *the singularity scheme has points $(x,z)$ satisfying $\\mu(x)=0$ and $zx=0$, with $z\\in\\mathfrak j$;*\n\n2.  *the support condition defined by $Y$ is the condition that the image of $z$ under $\\mathfrak j\\hookrightarrow\\widehat{\\mathfrak g}$ belongs to $Y$;*\n\n3.  *every evaluation representation of $K=\\widehat G^d$ is the bundle associated to its restriction along $J\\hookrightarrow K$; Frobenius has constant cyclic transport on these bundles;*\n\n4.  *after removing the scalars $q^{1/2}$ on deformation points and $q^{-1}$ on singular points, Frobenius is a linear automorphism of the moment-map data, normalizing $J$ and the framed representation labels. Its eigenvalues on these normalized linear data have modulus one.*\n\n*The meaning of Frobenius on Hom complexes in <span class=\"upright\">(iv)</span> is fixed explicitly in Lemma <a href=\"#mod:hom-action\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"mod:hom-action\">13</a> below.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-mod-quadratic-model",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"mod:model\" label=\"mod:model\"></span> The retained component $Z_\\sigma$, with its Frobenius action, evaluation bundles at the chosen cyclic tuple, and singular-support map, admits the model $$\\label{mod:quadratic-equation}\n Z_\\sigma\\simeq\n \\bigl[(\\mu^{-1}(0))^{\\wedge}_{\\mathrm{unst}}/J\\bigr].$$ Here $\\mathrm{unst}$ is the inverse image of the origin under $V\\to V/\\!/J$, and completion is along this invariant null fiber. In this model:*\n\n1.  *the singularity scheme has points $(x,z)$ satisfying $\\mu(x)=0$ and $zx=0$, with $z\\in\\mathfrak j$;*\n\n2.  *the support condition defined by $Y$ is the condition that the image of $z$ under $\\mathfrak j\\hookrightarrow\\widehat{\\mathfrak g}$ belongs to $Y$;*\n\n3.  *every evaluation representation of $K=\\widehat G^d$ is the bundle associated to its restriction along $J\\hookrightarrow K$; Frobenius has constant cyclic transport on these bundles;*\n\n4.  *after removing the scalars $q^{1/2}$ on deformation points and $q^{-1}$ on singular points, Frobenius is a linear automorphism of the moment-map data, normalizing $J$ and the framed representation labels. Its eigenvalues on these normalized linear data have modulus one.*\n\n*The meaning of Frobenius on Hom complexes in <span class=\"upright\">(iv)</span> is fixed explicitly in Lemma <a href=\"#mod:hom-action\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"mod:hom-action\">13</a> below.",
          "source_id": "PM-014.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "<span id=\"mod:model\" label=\"mod:model\"></span> The retained component $Z_\\sigma$, with its Frobenius action, evaluation bundles at the chosen cyclic tuple, and singular-support map, admits the model $$\\label{mod:quadratic-equation}\n Z_\\sigma\\simeq\n \\bigl[(\\mu^{-1}(0))^{\\wedge}_{\\mathrm{unst}}/J\\bigr].$$ Here $\\mathrm{unst}$ is the inverse image of the origin under $V\\to V/\\!/J$, and completion is along this invariant null fiber. In this model:*\n\n1.  *the singularity scheme has points $(x,z)$ satisfying $\\mu(x)=0$ and $zx=0$, with $z\\in\\mathfrak j$;*\n\n2.  *the support condition defined by $Y$ is the condition that the image of $z$ under $\\mathfrak j\\hookrightarrow\\widehat{\\mathfrak g}$ belongs to $Y$;*\n\n3.  *every evaluation representation of $K=\\widehat G^d$ is the bundle associated to its restriction along $J\\hookrightarrow K$; Frobenius has constant cyclic transport on these bundles;*\n\n4.  *after removing the scalars $q^{1/2}$ on deformation points and $q^{-1}$ on singular points, Frobenius is a linear automorphism of the moment-map data, normalizing $J$ and the framed representation labels. Its eigenvalues on these normalized linear data have modulus one.*\n\n*The meaning of Frobenius on Hom complexes in <span class=\"upright\">(iv)</span> is fixed explicitly in Lemma <a href=\"#mod:hom-action\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"mod:hom-action\">13</a> below.",
      "explanation": "Each retained fixed component has a derived moment-map presentation completed along the invariant null fiber, retaining evaluation bundles and the singular-support map.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "mod:moment",
          "exact_quote": "Choose such a component and its unique closed semisimple parameter $\\sigma$. Choose a pure Weil lift as in Theorem",
          "source_id": "PM-014.2"
        }
      ],

          "source_id": "PM-014.2"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-mod-quadratic-model"
    },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-mod-hom-action",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"mod:frobenius\" label=\"mod:frobenius\"></span> Choose structures $\\alpha_M:\\Phi M\\xrightarrow{\\sim}M$ on compact objects. On an arrow $h:M\\to N$ put $$\\begin{aligned}\n T(h)&=\\alpha_N\\,\\Phi(h)\\,\\alpha_M^{-1},\\label{mod:T}\\\\\n S(h)&=\\Phi^{-1}(\\alpha_N^{-1}h\\alpha_M)=T^{-1}(h).\n \\label{mod:S}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The action in the bilinear Hom pairing is $S$. On the quadratic model it gives absolute weight $-1$ to deformation functions and absolute weight $+2$ to degree-two obstruction operators. Thus its associated point weights on $(x,z)$ are $(1,-2)$, and $W(x,z)=\\langle z,\\mu(x)\\rangle$ has weight zero.*\n\n\n*The graph identification in <a href=\"#tr:graph-identification\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"tr:graph-identification\">[tr:graph-identification]</a> turns the original $\\Phi$-graph bimodule into the graph of the transported arrow action. In those coordinates the induced simultaneous bar actions $T$ and $S$ are inverse. If the paracyclic homotopy is $bH+Hb=1-T$, then $$\\label{mod:inverse-homotopy}\n b(-T^{-1}H)+(-T^{-1}H)b=1-S.$$ In particular using contracting $S$-weights does not replace $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}(\\Phi,-)$ by $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}(\\Phi^{-1},-)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-mod-compact-normalization",
        "exact_quote": "Let $J.e$ be a nilpotent orbit preserved by the normalized transporter in Theorem <a href=\"#mod:quadratic-model\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"mod:quadratic-model\">12</a>. After changing that transporter by an element of $J$, choose a triple $(e,h,f)$ in this orbit and write the normalized transporter as $$u=t\\,v_0.$$ There are simultaneous compact forms of the group and frame data such that:*\n\n1.  *$t$ belongs to a compact group, preserves the compact form $J_{\\mathrm{cpt}}$, and centralizes the triple;*\n\n2.  *$v_0$ is unipotent, centralizes $J$, and commutes with $t$;*\n\n3.  *for $L_e=Z_J(\\mathrm{SL}_{2,e})$, $L_{e,\\mathrm{cpt}}=L_e\\cap J_{\\mathrm{cpt}}$ is a maximal compact including its components, and every $k t v_0$, $k\\in L_{e,\\mathrm{cpt}}$, has modulus-one eigenvalues on all the normalized linear data.*\n\n\n*These assertions also hold on their tensor, symmetric, exterior, subquotient, and triple-weight constructions. After the orbit-fixing translation by $\\lambda_e(\\sqrt q)$, the semisimple value of the frame transport around the $d$-cycle belongs to $$\\label{mod:cyclic-compact-value}\n \\bigl\\{[\\lambda_e(q^{d/2})s]:\n         s\\in Z_{\\widehat G}(\\mathrm{SL}_{2,e})_{\\mathrm{cpt}}\\bigr\\}\n =B_{\\widehat G.e}.$$ This holds for every $k\\in L_{e,\\mathrm{cpt}}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-orb-koszul",
        "exact_quote": "The compact ind-coherent category on the uncompleted derived quotient $[\\mu^{-1}(0)/J]$ is the category of finite coherent graded $J$-equivariant factorizations of <a href=\"#orb:potential\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"orb:potential\">[orb:potential]</a>. Coherent singular support in a closed cone $\\Omega\\subset\\mathfrak j$ corresponds to local vanishing of the factorization off $V\\times\\Omega$. The completion in Theorem <a href=\"#mod:quadratic-model\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"mod:quadratic-model\">12</a> additionally imposes ordinary support on the inverse image of $0\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}R^J$.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.2"

        "anchor": "stmt-orb-localization",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\Omega'\\subset\\Omega$ be closed invariant cones.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-orb-weil-lifts",
        "exact_quote": "Every $M_P$ is the restriction of a compact object with $z$-support in $\\overline{J.e}$. If $P$ has a compatible locally finite cyclic structure, the extension can be given that structure. For a simple label fixed by Frobenius, a structure may be chosen whose triple-translated coefficient eigenvalues have modulus one. The same assertions hold for a power of Frobenius, for a finite cyclic list of labels, and after tensoring $P$ by any finite-dimensional $L_e$-representation with compatible cyclic structure, placed in lifted degree zero. Inverse structures are available for opposite category classes.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-orb-hom-calculation",
        "exact_quote": "For $P,P'\\in\\mathsf E_e$, the Hom complex between the orbit generators has a finite equivariant calculation with Euler terms $$\\label{orb:hom-euler}\n \\left[\n \\mathbb C[\\ker(e:V\\to V)]\\otimes\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_{D_e}(P,P')\\otimes\n \\Lambda^\\bullet(N_e[-1])\\otimes\n \\Lambda^\\bullet(\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lie}}(U_e)^*[-1])\n \\right]^{L_e}.$$ Here the coefficient factor has lifted degree zero; the shifts in the normal and Lie factors add one to the degrees in <a href=\"#orb:weight-table\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"orb:weight-table\">[orb:weight-table]</a>. The formula is an equality of alternating equivariant characters, and a finite-filtration calculation bounding weights, rather than an assertion that the Hom complex splits as the displayed tensor product.*\n\n\n*For pure coefficient normalizations, all nonconstant terms have strictly negative absolute $S_e$-weight. The weight-zero cohomology is $$\\label{orb:weight-zero-hom}\n H^\\bullet\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(M_P,M_{P'})_{\\mathrm{wt}=0}\n       =\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_{D_e,L_e}(P,P')[0],$$ with ordinary composition. Before the finite invariant calculation, weight pieces are finite-dimensional and their dimensions grow at most polynomially over bounded-length weight intervals. In particular, the character sums in <a href=\"#orb:hom-euler\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"orb:hom-euler\">[orb:hom-euler]</a> are absolutely convergent on every normalized compact $L_e$-coset, uniformly on that coset.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-orb-hom-calculation",
          "exact_quote": "For $P,P'\\in\\mathsf E_e$, the Hom complex between the orbit generators has a finite equivariant calculation with Euler terms $$\\label{orb:hom-euler}\n \\left[\n \\mathbb C[\\ker(e:V\\to V)]\\otimes\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_{D_e}(P,P')\\otimes\n \\Lambda^\\bullet(N_e[-1])\\otimes\n \\Lambda^\\bullet(\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lie}}(U_e)^*[-1])\n \\right]^{L_e}.$$ Here the coefficient factor has lifted degree zero; the shifts in the normal and Lie factors add one to the degrees in <a href=\"#orb:weight-table\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"orb:weight-table\">[orb:weight-table]</a>. The formula is an equality of alternating equivariant characters, and a finite-filtration calculation bounding weights, rather than an assertion that the Hom complex splits as the displayed tensor product.*\n\n\n*For pure coefficient normalizations, all nonconstant terms have strictly negative absolute $S_e$-weight. The weight-zero cohomology is $$\\label{orb:weight-zero-hom}\n H^\\bullet\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(M_P,M_{P'})_{\\mathrm{wt}=0}\n       =\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_{D_e,L_e}(P,P')[0],$$ with ordinary composition. Before the finite invariant calculation, weight pieces are finite-dimensional and their dimensions grow at most polynomially over bounded-length weight intervals. In particular, the character sums in <a href=\"#orb:hom-euler\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"orb:hom-euler\">[orb:hom-euler]</a> are absolutely convergent on every normalized compact $L_e$-coset, uniformly on that coset.",
          "source_id": "PM-014.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For $P,P'\\in\\mathsf E_e$, the Hom complex between the orbit generators has a finite equivariant calculation with Euler terms $$\\label{orb:hom-euler}\n \\left[\n \\mathbb C[\\ker(e:V\\to V)]\\otimes\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_{D_e}(P,P')\\otimes\n \\Lambda^\\bullet(N_e[-1])\\otimes\n \\Lambda^\\bullet(\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lie}}(U_e)^*[-1])\n \\right]^{L_e}.$$ Here the coefficient factor has lifted degree zero; the shifts in the normal and Lie factors add one to the degrees in <a href=\"#orb:weight-table\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"orb:weight-table\">[orb:weight-table]</a>. The formula is an equality of alternating equivariant characters, and a finite-filtration calculation bounding weights, rather than an assertion that the Hom complex splits as the displayed tensor product.*\n\n\n*For pure coefficient normalizations, all nonconstant terms have strictly negative absolute $S_e$-weight. The weight-zero cohomology is $$\\label{orb:weight-zero-hom}\n H^\\bullet\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(M_P,M_{P'})_{\\mathrm{wt}=0}\n       =\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_{D_e,L_e}(P,P')[0],$$ with ordinary composition. Before the finite invariant calculation, weight pieces are finite-dimensional and their dimensions grow at most polynomially over bounded-length weight intervals. In particular, the character sums in <a href=\"#orb:hom-euler\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"orb:hom-euler\">[orb:hom-euler]</a> are absolutely convergent on every normalized compact $L_e$-coset, uniformly on that coset.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-tr-invariant-support",
        "exact_quote": "For every Frobenius-stable conical $z$-support bound $\\Omega$, inclusion induces an isomorphism $$\\label{tr:remove-completion}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}(\\Phi,\\operatorname{Ind}\\mathscr B_{\\Omega,0})\n       \\xrightarrow{\\;\\sim\\;}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}(\\Phi,\\operatorname{Ind}\\mathscr B_\\Omega).$$ A compact Weil object on the right has a compact Weil lift on the left whose trace class maps to a nonzero scalar multiple of its class.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-tr-bar-contraction",
        "exact_quote": "Let a small generating differential graded category with an autoequivalence be modeled in complexes of locally finite cyclic representations. Suppose, after finite cyclic permutations of its labels and pure choices of their structures, its morphism cohomology has nonpositive absolute $S$-weights; composition adds weights. Suppose its weight-zero morphism cohomology is concentrated in degree zero and its additive idempotent completion is semisimple, with endomorphism algebra $\\mathbb C$ for every simple object. Then its twisted trace is the twisted trace of that split semisimple category. It is concentrated in degree zero, with one basis line for each fixed simple label. Nontrivially permuted simple labels contribute zero.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "exact_quote": "On a fixed retained spectral component, the trace of every closed invariant nilpotent support category is concentrated in degree zero. A finite filtration by invariant unions of orbits has the orbit traces as its actual successive quotients. Every trace class is a finite linear combination of classes of compact objects with Weil structures; those objects can be chosen with support in the prescribed bound. All assertions hold on the compact opposite side.*\n\n*After assembling retained components, these statements apply to $\\mathcal C_Y$ and $\\mathcal C$.       "hypotheses": [

          "anchor": "stmt-intro-characteristic",
          "exact_quote": "The following four conditions hold.\n\n1.  The Lie algebra $\\mathfrak h$ has an $H$-invariant nondegenerate symmetric bilinear form whose restriction to the center of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lie}}(M)$ is nondegenerate for every Levi subgroup $M$ of $H$.\n\n2.  For each Levi subgroup $M$ of $H$, including $H$ itself, and each maximal torus $T_M\\subset M$, restriction induces an isomorphism $$k_0[\\mathfrak m]^M\\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }\n    k_0[\\mathfrak t_M]^{W_M},\\qquad\n    \\mathfrak m=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lie}}(M),\\quad \\mathfrak t_M=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lie}}(T_M),\\quad\n    W_M=N_M(T_M)/T_M.$$\n\n3.  The scheme-theoretic centralizer in $H$ of every semisimple element of $\\mathfrak h$ is a Levi subgroup.\n\n4.  For every extension $K/k_0$ and every nilpotent $n\\in\\mathfrak h\\otimes_{k_0}K$, there is a parabolic subgroup $P\\subset H_K$, defined over $K$, such that $n\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lie}}(R_u(P))$.\n\nHere a Levi subgroup is a Levi factor of a parabolic subgroup. Semisimple means geometrically conjugate into the Lie algebra of a maximal torus; nilpotent means that the geometric adjoint orbit closure contains zero.",
          "source_id": "PM-014.2"
        }
      ],

        "exact_quote": "Let $N$ and $M$ be compact spectral objects with the structures just specified, and let $f_N$ and $g_M$ be their automorphic trace functions, where $M$ is transported through the categorical duality.         "exact_quote": "Let $O=J.e$ be a stable nilpotent orbit, closed in an invariant open subset of $\\mathfrak j$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Finitely supported automorphic function space** ([source](#pm-h-3)): For a characteristic-zero field $L$, let $$\mathcal A_L=L^{(S)}$$ be the space of finitely supported functions on $S$.
- **Canonical Arthur filtration** ([source](#intro:filtration)): Define $$\label{intro:filtration} \mathcal F_Y\mathcal A_E= \mathop{\mathrm{im}}\bigl[H^0\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\Phi,\mathcal C_Y)\longrightarrow\mathcal A_E\bigr].$$
- **Categorical trace** ([source](#intro:filtration)): For a dualizable differential graded category $\mathcal D$ and an endofunctor $T$, write $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(T,\mathcal D)$ for its categorical trace, a complex of vector spaces.
- **Automorphic bilinear pairing** ([source](#intro:pairing)): For $f,g\in\mathcal A_\mathbb C$, put $$\label{intro:pairing} [f,g]=\sum_{b\in S}\frac{f(b)g(b)}{\|\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(b)\|}.$$
- **Compact nilpotent spectral sets** ([source](#intro:compactsets)): Define $$\label{intro:compactsets} B_o=\bigl\{[\lambda_e(\sqrt r)s]: s\in Z_{\widehat G_\mathbb C}(\mathrm{SL}_{2,e})_{\mathrm{cpt}}\bigr\} \subset\widehat G_\mathbb C\mathbin{/\mkern-6mu/}\widehat G_\mathbb C, \qquad B_Y=\bigcup_{o\subset Y_\mathbb C}B_o.$$
- **Absolute weight** ([source](#mod:section)): An invertible linear operator has *absolute weight $w$* if all its eigenvalues have modulus $q^{w/2}$.
- **Quadratic moment map** ([source](#mod:moment)): The moment map is $$\label{mod:moment} \mu:V\longrightarrow\mathfrak j^*,\qquad \langle\mu(x),z\rangle=\tfrac12\omega(zx,x).$$
- **Curved module** ([source](#orb:potential)): A curved module has a differential $\delta$ of degree one satisfying $\delta^2=W$.
- **Lifted degree** ([source](#orb:weight-table)): We call the resulting degree on a fiber at $e$ the *lifted degree*.
- **Finite complex measure** ([source](#intro:detector)): A finite complex measure means one of finite total variation.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-pure-adjoint-eigenvalue-modulus | Absolute value of an adjoint cohomology Frobenius eigenvalue | = | $q^{i/2}$ | The specified cohomological range and chosen abstract coefficient isomorphism. |
| q-closed-point-norm | Closed-point norm parameter | = | $q^d$ | The degree of that closed point over the fixed finite field. |
| q-deformation-hom-weight | Absolute inverse-Hom weight of deformation functions | = | $-1$ | Deformation functions before triple translation. |
| q-obstruction-operator-hom-weight | Absolute inverse-Hom weight of obstruction operators | = | $+2$ | The bilinear inverse transport action before triple translation. |
| q-nonresonant-conjugacy-modulus | Eigenvalue modulus of the finite-jet conjugacy operator | = | $q^{-(n-1)/2}$ | Each successive generator order; no diagonalization is assumed. |
| q-orbit-deformation-function-weight | Absolute translated weight of a deformation function | = | $-1-i$ | The indicated triple-weight summand of the deformation space. |
| q-orbit-normal-tangent-weight | Absolute translated weight of a normal tangent | = | $-2+i$ | The indicated lowest-weight summand and translated inverse action. |
| q-orbit-unipotent-cochain-weight | Absolute translated weight of a unipotent Lie cochain factor | = | $-i$ | A positive triple-weight summand before its cohomological shift. |
| q-supported-character-summability | Sum of eigenvalue moduli in a polynomial-degree and normal-order term | ≤ | $C(m+1)^a(n+1)^b\rho_x^m\rho_z^n$ | Polynomial degree and normal order; the constants depend on the fixed objects and finite coefficient spaces. |
| q-supported-deformation-decay | Permitted deformation-degree contraction ratio | = | $q^{-1/2}$ | After finite coefficient spaces are absorbed into the prefactor. |
| q-supported-normal-decay | Permitted normal-order contraction ratio | = | $q^{-1}$ | After finite coefficient spaces are absorbed into the prefactor. |
| q-proper-boundary-dimension | Ambient nilpotent orbit dimension at a proper boundary point | < | $\dim(\widehat G.e)$ | Any proper boundary point of the chosen stabilizer orbit. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-restricted-equivalence** [claim](#stmt-inp-langlands) / [proof](#stmt-inp-langlands): Use the recorded restricted geometric Langlands input with its retained component union and compatibility assertions. -> A support-compatible and Frobenius-compatible spectral description preserving compact objects.
- **step-trace-function-comparison** [claim](#stmt-inp-trace) / [proof](#stmt-inp-trace): Apply the recorded ambient categorical trace and ordinary local-term comparison. -> The ambient trace identified with actual finitely supported automorphic trace functions.
- **step-perfect-bilinear-duality** [claim](#stmt-inp-duality) / [proof](#pm-h-8): Use nonstandard self-duality and compactness preservation under star tensor product, finite Hecke insertion and compact pushforward. -> Perfect scalar evaluation complexes realizing the bilinear stack-weighted pairing.
- **step-component-deformation-input** [claim](#stmt-inp-components) / [proof](#stmt-inp-components): Use the recorded component and deformation-theory input to obtain formal affine framed stages and the adjoint tangent complex. -> A unique closed semisimple orbit with reductive stabilizer and coherent finite-stage geometry.
- **step-pure-weil-parameter** [claim](#stmt-inp-pure-lift) / [proof](#stmt-inp-pure-lift): Choose the recorded pure semisimple Weil lift and use smooth proper curve purity and duality. -> Separated absolute Frobenius weights on tangent and obstruction spaces.
- **step-recover-finite-covariants** [claim](#stmt-mod-finite-covariants) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mod-finite-covariants): Combine finite covariant generation over the Artinian invariant ring, Krull intersection and exact reductive covariants. -> Eventual stabilization of every finite covariant test and rational recovery of modules and multiplication.
- **step-restore-invariant-base** [claim](#stmt-mod-reconstruction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mod-reconstruction): Recover derived orbit jets and mapping spaces through rational covariants, recover the invariant base through trivial covariants, and extend through derived Koszul base stages. -> A uniquely extended invariant-fiber map with full homotopy and finite-presentation data.
- **step-remove-higher-resonances** [claim](#stmt-mod-nonresonance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mod-nonresonance): Solve the invertible conjugacy equation on each finite jet order and pass to the generator-adic limit; compare differential weights. -> Linear transport and a quadratic minimal differential, without diagonalizing Jordan blocks.
- **step-build-quadratic-component** [claim](#stmt-mod-quadratic-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mod-quadratic-model): Construct an equivariant slice, identify the cup-bracket obstruction with the moment map, linearize transport, and restore the invariant base and framed evaluation data. -> The completed derived moment-map quotient with its singular-support map and cyclic evaluation bundles.
- **step-fix-inverse-arrow-action** [claim](#stmt-mod-hom-action) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mod-hom-action): Compute duality evaluation on arrows and identify inverse transport, including the graph-bimodule convention and inverse paracyclic homotopy. -> The contracting Hom action on the original Frobenius graph.
- **step-normalize-compact-data** [claim](#stmt-mod-compact-normalization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mod-compact-normalization): Separate Jordan factors, remove the inner unipotent action, choose compatible maximal compact forms and conjugate the triple compatibly with cyclic frames. -> Uniform modulus control under compact stabilizer twists and the required compact cyclic parameter value.
- **step-koszul-curved-model** [claim](#stmt-orb-koszul) / [proof](#proof-stmt-orb-koszul): Apply the graded derived-zero-locus Koszul equivalence and identify singular localization separately from ordinary formal-completion support. -> A coherent equivariant curved model with both support conditions retained.
- **step-supported-category-localization** [claim](#stmt-orb-localization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-orb-localization): Use finite Koszul projector stages and their ideal filtrations before their exact tensor action on curved modules. -> Compact localization and generation by coherent curved pushforwards, also with invariant-base support.
- **step-clifford-orbit-generators** [claim](#stmt-orb-generation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-orb-generation): Use derivatives of the potential to restrict support, stabilize the nondegenerate quadratic part, and average splittings in the Clifford coefficient category. -> Simple Clifford-labelled compact generators and a semisimple coefficient category.
- **step-actual-weil-closure-lifts** [claim](#stmt-orb-weil-lifts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-orb-weil-lifts): Extend coherent Clifford families over the degree component, induce along the proper incidence space, and retain algebraic cyclic structures through pushforward. -> Actual compact orbit-closure representatives with pure fixed-label normalizations and inverse structures.
- **step-orbit-hom-weight-calculation** [claim](#stmt-orb-hom-calculation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-orb-hom-calculation): Compute a finite Koszul filtration, contract the quadratic Clifford part, take finite unipotent Lie cochains and exact reductive invariants, and retain original parity. -> An alternating Euler-character calculation with strictly contracting nonconstant terms and ordinary weight-zero intertwiners.
- **step-remove-invariant-support-on-traces** [claim](#stmt-tr-invariant-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tr-invariant-support): Move central equations around the twisted bar, use their nonresonant transport, and apply local cohomology and finite invariant Koszul lifts. -> A trace isomorphism across ordinary support and actual lifts with nonzero constant pairing factors.
- **step-contract-twisted-bar** [claim](#stmt-tr-bar-contraction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tr-bar-contraction): Transport the original graph, invert the paracyclic defect algebraically on locally finite negative-weight blocks, and identify the residual semisimple trace. -> A degree-zero twisted trace with fixed-simple basis lines and no contribution from permuted labels.
- **step-compute-orbit-trace** [claim](#stmt-tr-orbit-trace) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tr-orbit-trace): Use Morita invariance on locally finite algebraic generator models and apply bar contraction; lift the resulting basis through incidence and invariant Koszul constructions. -> Orbit-trace concentration and actual compact Weil basis representatives on both categorical sides.
- **step-assemble-supported-traces** [claim](#stmt-tr-spanning) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tr-spanning): Induct through finite invariant orbit filtrations using localizing traces and actual quotient lifts, then take direct sums over retained fixed components. -> Injective supported trace images and finite compact Weil spanning for all automorphic functions and all tests.
- **step-hom-pairing-functions** [claim](#stmt-meas-hom-functions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-meas-hom-functions): Compare perfect compact duality evaluations and finite Hecke insertions with ordinary local terms and the stack-counting pairing. -> Every bilinear automorphic input-test pairing realized as a perfect Hom supertrace.
- **step-supported-character-convergence** [claim](#stmt-meas-supported-convergence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-meas-supported-convergence): Filter by supported local cohomology with normal symmetric powers and the normal determinant, then combine coherence with geometric weight decay. -> Uniformly absolutely convergent orbit characters additive through finite filtrations and equal to the final perfect supertrace.
- **step-compact-set-character-density** [claim](#stmt-desc-compactsets) / [proof](#proof-stmt-desc-compactsets): Recover triple cocharacters from positive polar parts, separate orbit labels, and apply the self-adjoint representation-character algebra on the compact union. -> Disjoint compact spectral sets, dense characters and invariance under inversion and finite central translates.
- **step-character-moment-uniqueness** [claim](#stmt-desc-unique-measure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-desc-unique-measure): Extend equality of character moments to equality of continuous functionals using uniform density and finite total variation. -> Uniqueness of a finite measure from all representation-character moments.
- **step-construct-common-support-measures** [claim](#stmt-meas-common-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-meas-common-support): Average supported character densities over compact stabilizers and push them to compact spectral sets using cyclic evaluation transport. -> Finite complex measures whose moments are the Hecke pairings and whose support lies in the common-support orbit union.
- **step-cancel-universal-density** [claim](#stmt-meas-cancel-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-meas-cancel-density): Express the universal density through contracting determinant factors and approximate its reciprocal by finite symmetric and exterior character combinations. -> Finite compact tests that approximate cancellation of the nonvanishing universal density.
- **step-detect-top-quotient** [claim](#stmt-meas-top-detection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-meas-top-detection): Assume all top masses vanish, cancel the density by uniform approximation, and use the semisimple coefficient pairing to isolate a nonzero simple coefficient. -> An actual finite compact test with nonzero top-orbit total mass.
- **step-separate-proper-boundaries** [claim](#stmt-meas-boundary-rank) / [proof](#proof-stmt-meas-boundary-rank): Split the ambient adjoint representation equivariantly; combine strict stabilizer-orbit rank drop with upper semicontinuity on the complement. -> Strict ambient orbit-dimension drop and disjoint top and boundary compact spectral sets.
- **step-prove-bilinear-detector** [claim](#stmt-meas-detector) / [proof](#pm-h-29): Construct measures from finite compact expansions, then choose the first nonzero orbit quotient outside the bound and exclude all boundary cancellation. -> The equivalence between filtration membership and support of every finitely supported bilinear test measure.
- **step-coefficient-independence** [claim](#stmt-desc-coefficient-independence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-desc-coefficient-independence): Compare rational point-function coordinates, normalized Hecke operations and pinned orbit labels; absorb square-root and contragredient changes by set-preserving symmetries. -> A transported filtration independent of the coefficient isomorphism and invariant under all rational coefficient automorphisms.
- **step-finite-coordinate-descent** [claim](#stmt-desc-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-desc-descent): Identify the automorphism fixed field, use uniqueness of reduced row-echelon bases on finite coordinate sets, and pass to their directed union. -> A rational subspace whose scalar extension is the given invariant subspace.
- **step-rational-arthur-filtration** [claim](#stmt-intro-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-desc-descent-2): Apply finite-coordinate descent to the coefficient-invariant Arthur filtration with its point-function basis. -> The claimed rational scalar-extension isomorphism on the full finitely supported function space.
- **step-cuspidal-orbit-comparison** [claim](#stmt-desc-cuspidal-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-desc-cuspidal-comparison): Group cuspidal Hecke eigenspaces by their one-place Satake values, identify the detector with the finite atomic measure, and descend the orbit-labelled intersection. -> The rational and coefficient-field cuspidal filtration intersections equal the prescribed sums of orbit summands, including extreme supports.

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-mod-quadratic-model",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"mod:model\" label=\"mod:model\"></span> The retained component $Z_\\sigma$, with its Frobenius action, evaluation bundles at the chosen cyclic tuple, and singular-support map, admits the model $$\\label{mod:quadratic-equation}\n Z_\\sigma\\simeq\n \\bigl[(\\mu^{-1}(0))^{\\wedge}_{\\mathrm{unst}}/J\\bigr].$$ Here $\\mathrm{unst}$ is the inverse image of the origin under $V\\to V/\\!/J$, and completion is along this invariant null fiber. In this model:*\n\n1.  *the singularity scheme has points $(x,z)$ satisfying $\\mu(x)=0$ and $zx=0$, with $z\\in\\mathfrak j$;*\n\n2.  *the support condition defined by $Y$ is the condition that the image of $z$ under $\\mathfrak j\\hookrightarrow\\widehat{\\mathfrak g}$ belongs to $Y$;*\n\n3.  *every evaluation representation of $K=\\widehat G^d$ is the bundle associated to its restriction along $J\\hookrightarrow K$; Frobenius has constant cyclic transport on these bundles;*\n\n4.  *after removing the scalars $q^{1/2}$ on deformation points and $q^{-1}$ on singular points, Frobenius is a linear automorphism of the moment-map data, normalizing $J$ and the framed representation labels. Its eigenvalues on these normalized linear data have modulus one.*\n\n*The meaning of Frobenius on Hom complexes in <span class=\"upright\">(iv)</span> is fixed explicitly in Lemma <a href=\"#mod:hom-action\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"mod:hom-action\">13</a> below.",
          "source_id": "PM-014.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-build-quadratic-component",
      "inputs": [
        "Formal component geometry",
        "Pure tangent and obstruction weights",
        "Invariant symplectic pairing"
      ],
      "method": "Construct an equivariant slice, identify the cup-bracket obstruction with the moment map, linearize transport, and restore the invariant base and framed evaluation data.",
      "output": "The completed derived moment-map quotient with its singular-support map and cyclic evaluation bundles.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-mod-quadratic-model"
    },

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-mod-hom-action",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-perfect-bilinear-duality",
        "step-build-quadratic-component"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-mod-hom-action",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"mod:frobenius\" label=\"mod:frobenius\"></span> Choose structures $\\alpha_M:\\Phi M\\xrightarrow{\\sim}M$ on compact objects. On an arrow $h:M\\to N$ put $$\\begin{aligned}\n T(h)&=\\alpha_N\\,\\Phi(h)\\,\\alpha_M^{-1},\\label{mod:T}\\\\\n S(h)&=\\Phi^{-1}(\\alpha_N^{-1}h\\alpha_M)=T^{-1}(h).\n \\label{mod:S}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The action in the bilinear Hom pairing is $S$. On the quadratic model it gives absolute weight $-1$ to deformation functions and absolute weight $+2$ to degree-two obstruction operators. Thus its associated point weights on $(x,z)$ are $(1,-2)$, and $W(x,z)=\\langle z,\\mu(x)\\rangle$ has weight zero.*\n\n\n*The graph identification in <a href=\"#tr:graph-identification\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"tr:graph-identification\">[tr:graph-identification]</a> turns the original $\\Phi$-graph bimodule into the graph of the transported arrow action. In those coordinates the induced simultaneous bar actions $T$ and $S$ are inverse. If the paracyclic homotopy is $bH+Hb=1-T$, then $$\\label{mod:inverse-homotopy}\n b(-T^{-1}H)+(-T^{-1}H)b=1-S.$$ In particular using contracting $S$-weights does not replace $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}(\\Phi,-)$ by $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}(\\Phi^{-1},-)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-014.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-fix-inverse-arrow-action",
      "inputs": [
        "Compact Weil structures",
        "Nonstandard duality",
        "The quadratic model"

      "method": "Compute duality evaluation on arrows and identify inverse transport, including the graph-bimodule convention and inverse paracyclic homotopy.",
      "output": "The contracting Hom action on the original Frobenius graph.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-mod-hom-action"
    },

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-mod-compact-normalization",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-mod-compact-normalization",
          "exact_quote": "Let $J.e$ be a nilpotent orbit preserved by the normalized transporter in Theorem <a href=\"#mod:quadratic-model\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"mod:quadratic-model\">12</a>. After changing that transporter by an element of $J$, choose a triple $(e,h,f)$ in this orbit and write the normalized transporter as $$u=t\\,v_0.$$ There are simultaneous compact forms of the group and frame data such that:*\n\n1.  *$t$ belongs to a compact group, preserves the compact form $J_{\\mathrm{cpt}}$, and centralizes the triple;*\n\n2.  *$v_0$ is unipotent, centralizes $J$, and commutes with $t$;*\n\n3.  *for $L_e=Z_J(\\mathrm{SL}_{2,e})$, $L_{e,\\mathrm{cpt}}=L_e\\cap J_{\\mathrm{cpt}}$ is a maximal compact including its components, and every $k t v_0$, $k\\in L_{e,\\mathrm{cpt}}$, has modulus-one eigenvalues on all the normalized linear data.*\n\n\n*These assertions also hold on their tensor, symmetric, exterior, subquotient, and triple-weight constructions. After the orbit-fixing translation by $\\lambda_e(\\sqrt q)$, the semisimple value of the frame transport around the $d$-cycle belongs to $$\\label{mod:cyclic-compact-value}\n \\bigl\\{[\\lambda_e(q^{d/2})s]:\n         s\\in Z_{\\widehat G}(\\mathrm{SL}_{2,e})_{\\mathrm{cpt}}\\bigr\\}\n =B_{\\widehat G.e}.$$ This holds for every $k\\in L_{e,\\mathrm{cpt}}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-014.2"
        }
      ],

      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-mod-compact-normalization"

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-orb-hom-calculation",
          "exact_quote": "For $P,P'\\in\\mathsf E_e$, the Hom complex between the orbit generators has a finite equivariant calculation with Euler terms $$\\label{orb:hom-euler}\n \\left[\n \\mathbb C[\\ker(e:V\\to V)]\\otimes\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_{D_e}(P,P')\\otimes\n \\Lambda^\\bullet(N_e[-1])\\otimes\n \\Lambda^\\bullet(\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lie}}(U_e)^*[-1])\n \\right]^{L_e}.$$ Here the coefficient factor has lifted degree zero; the shifts in the normal and Lie factors add one to the degrees in <a href=\"#orb:weight-table\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"orb:weight-table\">[orb:weight-table]</a>. The formula is an equality of alternating equivariant characters, and a finite-filtration calculation bounding weights, rather than an assertion that the Hom complex splits as the displayed tensor product.*\n\n\n*For pure coefficient normalizations, all nonconstant terms have strictly negative absolute $S_e$-weight. The weight-zero cohomology is $$\\label{orb:weight-zero-hom}\n H^\\bullet\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(M_P,M_{P'})_{\\mathrm{wt}=0}\n       =\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_{D_e,L_e}(P,P')[0],$$ with ordinary composition. Before the finite invariant calculation, weight pieces are finite-dimensional and their dimensions grow at most polynomially over bounded-length weight intervals. In particular, the character sums in <a href=\"#orb:hom-euler\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"orb:hom-euler\">[orb:hom-euler]</a> are absolutely convergent on every normalized compact $L_e$-coset, uniformly on that coset.",
          "source_id": "PM-014.2"
        }

      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-orb-hom-calculation"
    },
