<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-rational-arthur-filtration**: The canonical Arthur filtration descends to rational finitely supported automorphic functions, including the noncuspidal part, under the stated characteristic hypotheses.
- **invariant-supported-trace-injection**: Supported traces are concentrated in degree zero, inclusion of closed invariant support bounds induces an injection, and actual compact Weil trace classes span each prescribed support image.
- **invariant-original-frobenius-graph**: The inverse transport action supplies contracting Hom weights while the categorical trace continues to use the original Frobenius graph.
- **invariant-bilinear-support-detector**: Hecke matrix coefficients determine a unique finite complex Borel measure, and filtration membership is equivalent to its prescribed compact spectral support for every finitely supported test.
- **invariant-density-and-boundary-separation**: The top-orbit density is nowhere zero, nonzero quotient classes admit compact detecting tests, and proper boundary orbits have strictly smaller ambient orbit dimension.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-function-support-substitution**: Interpret the nilpotent support bound as a restriction on which bundle classes may have nonzero function values. -> This substitutes ordinary support on bundle classes for a condition on spectral singular directions.
- **anti-use-positive-measure-assumption**: Treat the detector measures as positive probability distributions or replace the bilinear pairing by a Hermitian pairing. -> Positivity and complex conjugation would change the scalar evaluation and invalidate the cancellation argument.
- **anti-use-inverse-functor-substitution**: Replace the categorical Frobenius functor by its inverse because the inverse arrow action contracts. -> The Hom action and the categorical endofunctor are different data; changing the endofunctor changes the trace being calculated.
- **anti-use-euler-character-as-splitting**: Read the orbit Hom Euler terms as a direct tensor-product decomposition of the Hom complex. -> An alternating character identity does not recover the differentials or a splitting of the underlying complex.
- **anti-use-ordinary-orbit-restriction**: Compute boundary contributions by restricting differential Hom to each orbit and adding the restrictions. -> Ordinary restriction omits the local-cohomology normal contributions required for the measure calculation.
- **anti-use-finite-tests-as-universal-certificate**: Certify the full rationality theorem by checking finitely many functions, Hecke characters, or coefficient automorphisms. -> Such checks do not establish the universal measure support condition or invariance under every coefficient automorphism.
- **anti-use-ramified-cuspidal-extension**: Extend the cuspidal comparison to arbitrary ramified level or discard its characteristic assumptions. -> The comparison identifies the specified unramified cuspidal subspace under the same characteristic conditions; broader cases are not established here.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The paper proves rationality and a universal support criterion for the full space of finitely supported automorphic functions, rather than a finite computational problem with an executable input representation. Its detector requires every finitely supported test, and its descent uses invariance under every coefficient automorphism. The finite-coordinate argument is conditional on that universal invariance; checking a finite matrix would not certify the paper's theorem. The source explicitly states: “The sum is a direct sum, including when there are infinitely many components.” It also states: “This is legitimate integration against a fixed continuous function, not a limit of automorphic functions.” Finite calculations cannot replace these categorical and measure-theoretic arguments.

| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The supported categorical trace defines a subspace of automorphic functions. It supplies no software architecture, program transformation, or executable implementation contract. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The moment-map model describes a derived geometric fiber. Its deformation variables are not a simulation state with specified evolution rules. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Equivariant reconstruction recovers formal geometric data and full mapping spaces under specific hypotheses. The paper supplies no computational procedure for a general symbolic reconstruction tool. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The support detector characterizes filtration membership through representation moments and every finitely supported test. It does not give a finite decision procedure for that membership problem. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The component decomposition is a direct sum of categorical traces, including for infinitely many components. It provides no storage representation or data-structure operations. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The geometric convergence estimate bounds eigenvalue contributions in a character calculation. It does not specify hardware workloads, resource consumption, or acceleration methods. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Coefficient independence concerns automorphisms of an abstract coefficient field and rational descent. It establishes no cryptographic hardness assumption or security property. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The detector uses algebraic representation characters and spherical Hecke operations. The paper does not construct a learning rule or connect its measures to embedding similarity. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The strict drop of ambient orbit dimension separates boundary contributions in a proof. It supplies neither an optimisation objective nor an algorithm for scheduling or resource allocation. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Finite cyclic permutations describe the action on categorical labels. Their trace calculation does not model distributed communication, failures, or agreement protocols. |
| other (Geometric representation theory) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The paper constructs orbit generators as curved pushforwards with Clifford-module coefficients and extends them to compact objects on orbit closures. This provides a mathematical modelling contract for supported categories; adaptation to a computational representation would require further work. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-curve-and-group-setting** (hypothesis): The rationality assertion concerns a smooth projective geometrically connected curve over the finite field and a split connected semisimple group. Blocked use: ['Applying the main theorem to nonprojective curves or arbitrary nonsplit or nonsemisimple groups.']
- **limit-invariant-form-on-levi-centers** (hypothesis): The required invariant bilinear form must remain nondegenerate on the Lie-algebra center of every Levi subgroup. Blocked use: ['Applying the characteristic-dependent geometric inputs when the invariant form or its required restrictions degenerate.']
- **limit-levi-invariant-restriction** (hypothesis): Restriction of invariant functions must give the specified isomorphism for every Levi subgroup and maximal torus. Blocked use: ['Treating an unchecked restriction map on Levi invariants as satisfying the characteristic assumptions.']
- **limit-scheme-theoretic-centralizers** (hypothesis): The characteristic assumptions require scheme-theoretic centralizers of semisimple Lie-algebra elements to be Levi subgroups. Blocked use: ['Replacing the centralizer hypothesis by a statement only about its geometric points.']
- **limit-nilpotents-over-extensions** (hypothesis): The parabolic containment condition must hold for nilpotents over every extension of the algebraically closed base field. Blocked use: ['Checking parabolic containment only for nilpotents defined over the initial field.']
- **limit-finite-function-support** (formal_scope): The ambient automorphic space consists of finitely supported functions on bundle isomorphism classes. This does not assert that the entire ambient vector space is finite-dimensional. Blocked use: ['Extending the result to arbitrary infinite-support functions without a separate convergence and trace comparison.']
- **limit-singular-support-meaning** (formal_scope): A nilpotent support bound constrains spectral singular directions rather than the bundle classes on which a function is nonzero. Blocked use: ['Computing an Arthur filtration step by deleting point-function coordinates according to a nilpotent orbit label.']
- **limit-rational-locally-finite-reconstruction** (hypothesis): Recovery from closed-orbit completion uses reductive equivariance, finite generation, an Artinian invariant ring and a unique closed orbit. The rational locally finite part is essential. Blocked use: ['Recovering the original algebra from the unrestricted vector-space completion.', 'Applying finite covariant stabilization without its invariant-ring and closed-orbit hypotheses.']
- **limit-derived-formal-reconstruction** (nonconstructive): Restoring the invariant base recovers full mapping spaces and coherent homotopies through derived formal stages. It is not supplied as a bounded finite reconstruction procedure. Blocked use: ['Replacing derived Koszul stages by ordinary quotients on the strength of nilpotence alone.', 'Claiming a uniform finite jet bound for recovering all cohomological degrees and representation tests.']
- **limit-formal-nonresonance** (hypothesis): Formal linearization requires the stated generator degrees, separated absolute weights, continuity and minimality; its coordinate changes converge in the generator-adic topology. Blocked use: ['Inferring linearization for resonant weights.', 'Interpreting the formal coordinate change as a terminating finite computation.']
- **limit-derived-fiber-and-invariant-completion** (formal_scope): The moment-map zero fiber is derived, and the component model is completed along the invariant null fiber rather than merely the closed orbit. Blocked use: ['Replacing the derived zero fiber by its classical zero locus.', 'Discarding nonclosed points of the invariant null fiber.']
- **limit-hom-action-and-graph** (formal_scope): The contracting action on arrows is inverse transport, while the categorical trace retains the original Frobenius endofunctor through the graph identification. Blocked use: ['Substituting the inverse categorical endofunctor when using contracting Hom weights.']
- **limit-compact-twists-and-parity** (hypothesis): Orbit character estimates require simultaneous compact normalization after stabilizer twists. The translated grading must retain the original parity convention. Blocked use: ['Using modulus control before twisting as sufficient for every compact stabilizer twist.', 'Changing a commuting polynomial variable into an exterior variable because its lifted degree is odd.']
- **limit-orbit-generation-and-actual-lifts** (formal_scope): Orbit generators live in an open where the orbit is closed. Compatible cyclic structures and actual compact orbit-closure extensions are retained before using their trace classes globally. Blocked use: ['Using a formal orbit label without a compact extension as an established global automorphic test.', 'Assuming that an arbitrary coefficient structure has the required compatible cyclic action.']
- **limit-euler-character-not-complex-splitting** (formal_scope): The orbit Hom formula gives alternating equivariant characters and a finite-filtration weight calculation; it does not split the Hom complex. Blocked use: ['Reading the displayed Euler terms as an isomorphism of complexes.', 'Discarding possible differentials because the Euler character is known.']
- **limit-invariant-support-lifts-scalars** (formal_scope): Removing invariant null-fiber support gives an isomorphism on traces. Actual compact Weil lifts represent classes up to nonzero scalar multiples, with constant nonzero factors in orbitwise Hom contributions. Blocked use: ['Treating equality of supported and unsupported traces as equality of the underlying categories.', 'Assuming an unrescaled compact lift has exactly the original trace class.']
- **limit-locally-finite-bar-contraction** (hypothesis): The bar contraction requires locally finite cyclic chain models, nonpositive morphism-cohomology weights and a split semisimple weight-zero category with scalar simple endomorphisms. Blocked use: ['Applying the contraction from numerical cohomological weight bounds alone.', 'Replacing the direct-sum bar realization by an unrestricted product.', 'Counting labels fixed only by a power as fixed labels of the original functor.']
- **limit-finite-spanning-and-component-sums** (formal_scope): Compact Weil classes span through finite linear combinations, and retained fixed components assemble by a direct sum. Blocked use: ['Allowing an individual automorphic vector to acquire infinitely many component contributions.', 'Inferring support injectivity from concentration of the full category alone.']
- **limit-bilinear-complex-measures** (formal_scope): The detector uses a bilinear pairing and finite complex measures of finite total variation. Positivity and Hermitian conjugation are not part of the result. Blocked use: ['Treating detector measures as positive probability distributions.', 'Replacing the bilinear pairing by a Hermitian pairing.']
- **limit-supported-convergence** (hypothesis): Orbit contributions use local cohomology with supports and uniform absolute convergence on the compact transporter coset. Ordinary orbit restriction does not supply the boundary calculation. Blocked use: ['Summing ordinary restrictions to boundary orbits as a replacement for supported local cohomology.', 'Interchanging the character sums and compact averaging without the supplied convergence argument.']
- **limit-common-support-and-boundary-separation** (formal_scope): Input-test measures are bounded by common singular support. Detection of a top quotient uses a closure-supported test and strict ambient orbit-dimension drop to exclude proper-boundary cancellation. Blocked use: ['Assigning measure mass using only the input support while ignoring the test.', 'Inferring top detection without distinguishing the ambient compact sets of proper boundaries.']
- **limit-uniform-density-approximation** (approximation): The reciprocal universal density is obtained as a uniform limit of finite character combinations. Existence of a finite detecting test does not supply a finite search bound or approximation threshold. Blocked use: ['Claiming that an arbitrarily chosen finite character truncation already detects every nonzero quotient class.', 'Replacing the argument by a limit of automorphic functions.']
- **limit-universal-detector** (nonconstructive): Filtration membership is characterized using every finitely supported test and representation-character moments. The theorem does not furnish a finite membership decision procedure. Blocked use: ['Certifying full filtration membership from an arbitrary finite family of tests or moments.']
- **limit-full-automorphism-invariance** (hypothesis): Rational descent assumes an algebraically closed coefficient extension, a specified rational basis and invariance under every coefficient automorphism fixing the rational field. Blocked use: ['Using finite row reduction as proof of the universal automorphism-invariance premise.', 'Replacing the algebraically closed extension by an arbitrary field without a new descent argument.']
- **limit-coefficient-comparison-scope** (formal_scope): Comparisons across coefficient primes match closed nilpotent supports through the pinned split dual group and identify rational function subspaces. Blocked use: ['Claiming a coefficient-prime identification of spectral support categories or individual eigenobjects.']
- **limit-unramified-cuspidal-common-scope** (formal_scope): The comparison with rational cuspidal orbit summands concerns unramified level under the same curve, split connected semisimple group and characteristic assumptions. It includes the zero cusp space and empty or full support. Blocked use: ['Extending the cuspidal orbit-summand comparison to ramified level.', 'Adding a nonzero cusp-space requirement or excluding extreme supports.']
- **limit-no-additional-cuspidality-or-rational-point** (edge_case): The main rationality assertion does not impose cuspidality or Hecke-finiteness and does not require a rational point on the curve. Blocked use: ['Restricting the main theorem to cuspidal or Hecke-finite functions.', 'Adding existence of a rational point as a prerequisite.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Under the characteristic hypotheses of restricted geometric Langlands theory, we prove that the canonical Arthur filtration on finitely supported unramified automorphic functions is defined over ℚ for split connected semisimple groups. The result includes the noncuspidal part and every closed invariant nilpotent support, establishing the rationality conjecture of Gaitsgory–Lafforgue–Raskin in this setting.

<a id="pm-h-2"></a>
### Introduction

The canonical Arthur filtration organizes unramified automorphic functions by nilpotent orbits in the Langlands dual Lie algebra. Its definition uses singular support in a category of $\ell$-adic sheaves, whereas the underlying space of finitely supported functions has a natural rational form. The rationality question asks whether these two structures are compatible.

Arthur’s conjectures organize departures from temperedness by an additional algebraic $\mathrm{SL}_2$ factor in an automorphic parameter <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Arthur1989](#ref-Arthur1989), Sections 4 and 8]. In geometric Langlands, Arinkin–Gaitsgory introduced nilpotent singular support to encode this Arthur direction <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[ArinkinGaitsgorySingularSupport](#ref-ArinkinGaitsgorySingularSupport), Sections 1.1.2–1.1.6]. The restricted local-system stack and nilpotent automorphic category of Arinkin–Gaitsgory–Kazhdan–Raskin–Rozenblyum–Varshavsky provide the corresponding setting over a finite field <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[AGKRRV1](#ref-AGKRRV1)].

Gaitsgory–Lafforgue–Raskin formulate this question, jointly with Kazhdan, as the assertion that their canonical filtration is defined over $\mathbb Q$ <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[GLR](#ref-GLR), Section 2.7, Conjecture 2.7.3]. The construction and its relation to Arthur’s and Ramanujan’s conjectures are discussed in <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[GLR](#ref-GLR), [Raskin](#ref-Raskin)]. Restricted geometric Langlands theory supplies the spectral description of the support categories, and the trace-of-Frobenius theorem identifies the ambient trace with automorphic functions <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[AGKRRV1](#ref-AGKRRV1), [AGKRRV3](#ref-AGKRRV3), [GR](#ref-GR)]. These results make rationality a precise question about the images of supported categorical traces.

We prove the rationality assertion for split connected semisimple groups under the explicit characteristic assumptions below. Thus the result positively resolves Conjecture 2.7.3 of <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[GLR](#ref-GLR)] in this setting. It applies to the entire space of finitely supported functions, including its noncuspidal part.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Hypotheses and the filtration

Let $X$ be a smooth projective geometrically connected curve over $\mathbb F_q$, and let $G/\mathbb F_q$ be split, connected and semisimple. Put $k_0=\overline{\mathbb F}_q$, $H=G_{k_0}$ and $\mathfrak h=\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(H)$.

<a id="stmt-intro-characteristic"></a>
<div id="intro:characteristic" class="assumption">

**Assumption 1**. The following four conditions hold.

1.  The Lie algebra $\mathfrak h$ has an $H$-invariant nondegenerate symmetric bilinear form whose restriction to the center of $\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(M)$ is nondegenerate for every Levi subgroup $M$ of $H$.

2.  For each Levi subgroup $M$ of $H$, including $H$ itself, and each maximal torus $T_M\subset M$, restriction induces an isomorphism $$k_0[\mathfrak m]^M\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }
    k_0[\mathfrak t_M]^{W_M},\qquad
    \mathfrak m=\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(M),\quad \mathfrak t_M=\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(T_M),\quad
    W_M=N_M(T_M)/T_M.$$

3.  The scheme-theoretic centralizer in $H$ of every semisimple element of $\mathfrak h$ is a Levi subgroup.

4.  For every extension $K/k_0$ and every nilpotent $n\in\mathfrak h\otimes_{k_0}K$, there is a parabolic subgroup $P\subset H_K$, defined over $K$, such that $n\in\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(R_u(P))$.

Here a Levi subgroup is a Levi factor of a parabolic subgroup. Semisimple means geometrically conjugate into the Lie algebra of a maximal torus; nilpotent means that the geometric adjoint orbit closure contains zero.

</div>

Fix a prime $\ell\ne\operatorname{char}(\mathbb F_q)$ and set $E=\overline{\mathbb Q}_{\ell}$. Write $S$ for the set of isomorphism classes of $G$-bundles on $X$ over $\mathbb F_q$. For a characteristic-zero field $L$, let $$\mathcal A_L=L^{(S)}$$ be the space of finitely supported functions on $S$. Its distinguished basis consists of the point functions. In particular $\mathcal A_E=E\otimes_\mathbb Q\mathcal A_\mathbb Q$.

Fix the pinned split $\mathbb Q$-form of the Langlands dual group and write $\widehat G/E$ for its scalar extension, with Lie algebra $\widehat{\mathfrak g}$ and nilpotent cone $\widehat{\mathcal N}$. All later coefficient identifications use this same pinned form. On $\overline X=X\times_{\mathbb F_q}k_0$ put $$\mathcal C=\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Nilp}}}(\mathop{\mathrm{Bun}}_G(\overline X),E),\qquad
\Phi=(\mathrm{Frob}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Bun}}})_*.$$ The subscript denotes singular support in the global nilpotent cone, the zero fiber of the Hitchin map. We use geometric Frobenius and its pushforward on the automorphic category.

For a closed $\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}(\widehat G)$-invariant subset $Y\subset\widehat{\mathcal N}$, let $\mathcal C_Y\subset\mathcal C$ be the derived-Satake support category. More explicitly, the restricted geometric Langlands equivalence identifies <a id="intro:restricted-equivalence"></a>$$\label{intro:restricted-equivalence}
\mathcal C\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{IndCoh}}_{\widehat{\mathcal N}}(Z),\qquad
Z={}^{\prime}\!\mathop{\mathrm{LocSys}}_{\widehat G}^{\mathrm{restr}}(\overline X),$$ and $\mathcal C_Y$ corresponds to $\mathop{\mathrm{IndCoh}}_Y(Z)$. The prime denotes the union of connected components occurring in that equivalence; it is retained throughout. A singular point is a pair $(\sigma,A)$ with $A$ a horizontal section of the adjoint local system, after identifying $\widehat{\mathfrak g}^*$ with $\widehat{\mathfrak g}$ by an invariant form. The support condition requires $A$ to take values in $Y$. Thus $Y$ is a condition on singular directions, not on the support of a function on $S$.

<a id="intro:filtration"></a>
For a dualizable differential graded category $\mathcal D$ and an endofunctor $T$, write $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(T,\mathcal D)$ for its categorical trace, a complex of vector spaces. It is Hochschild homology with coefficients in the graph bimodule of $T$. The trace/functions isomorphism gives $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\Phi,\mathcal C)\simeq\mathcal A_E$, with the latter in degree zero. Define $$\label{intro:filtration}
\mathcal F_Y\mathcal A_E=
\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\bigl[H^0\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\Phi,\mathcal C_Y)\longrightarrow\mathcal A_E\bigr].$$ This image definition does not assume concentration of the supported trace. The required concentration and injectivity statements will be proved in Section <a href="#tr:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="tr:section">5</a>.

<a id="stmt-intro-main"></a>
<div id="intro:main" class="theorem">

<a id="intro:main-map"></a>
**Theorem 2**. *Under Assumption <a href="#intro:characteristic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="intro:characteristic">1</a>, for every prime $\ell\ne\operatorname{char}(\mathbb F_q)$ and every closed invariant $Y\subset\widehat{\mathcal N}$, the natural map $$\label{intro:main-map}
E\otimes_\mathbb Q\bigl(\mathcal A_\mathbb Q\cap\mathcal F_Y\mathcal A_E\bigr)
\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathcal F_Y\mathcal A_E$$ is an isomorphism. The intersection is taken inside $\mathcal A_E$.*

</div>

No Hecke-finiteness or cuspidality condition is imposed. The theorem does not require $X(\mathbb F_q)$ to be nonempty. These rational filtration subspaces are also independent of $\ell$, with the closed invariant supports matched through the pinned split dual group, as shown in Section <a href="#desc:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="desc:section">7</a>. On the unramified cuspidal subspace, Corollary <a href="#desc:cuspidal-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="desc:cuspidal-comparison">35</a> further identifies this filtration with the sums of the rational cuspidal Arthur summands of <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[CuspidalArthur](#ref-CuspidalArthur), Theorem 1.1] whose orbit labels lie in $Y$.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### A bilinear detection criterion

The proof gives a characterization after every abstract field isomorphism $\iota:E\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb C$. Such isomorphisms are used without prescribing their restriction to the algebraic numbers. Transport all coefficient-linear categories and spaces by $\iota$.

<a id="intro:compactsets"></a>
Fix a closed point $v$ of $X$ of degree $d$, and put $r=q^d$. For a nilpotent orbit $o\subset\widehat{\mathfrak g}_\mathbb C$, choose an $\mathrm{SL}_2$-homomorphism associated with a triple for $e\in o$, and write $\lambda_e$ for its diagonal cocharacter, so $e$ has weight two. Define $$\label{intro:compactsets}
B_o=\bigl\{[\lambda_e(\sqrt r)s]:
s\in Z_{\widehat G_\mathbb C}(\mathrm{SL}_{2,e})_{\mathrm{cpt}}\bigr\}
\subset\widehat G_\mathbb C\mathbin{/\mkern-6mu/}\widehat G_\mathbb C,
\qquad
B_Y=\bigcup_{o\subset Y_\mathbb C}B_o.$$ Here $\sqrt r$ is positive, $[\ ]$ denotes semisimple conjugacy value, and the compact form includes the components of the triple centralizer. The sets $B_o$, as in <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[GLR](#ref-GLR), Sections 3.1.1–3.1.3], do not depend on these choices, are compact and pairwise disjoint. Their elementary properties are proved in Lemma <a href="#desc:compactsets" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="desc:compactsets">31</a>.

<a id="intro:pairing"></a>
For a finite-dimensional algebraic representation $D$ of $\widehat G_\mathbb C$, let $T_D$ be the spherical Hecke operator at $v$, with the positive square-root Satake normalization. For $f,g\in\mathcal A_\mathbb C$, put $$\label{intro:pairing}
[f,g]=\sum_{b\in S}\frac{f(b)g(b)}{|\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(b)|}.$$ This is a bilinear pairing. The automorphism groups over the finite field are finite, and the sum is finite.

<a id="intro:moments"></a>
<a id="intro:detector"></a>
The key result, Theorem <a href="#meas:detector" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="meas:detector">24</a>, says that for each $f,g$ there is a unique finite complex Borel measure $\nu_{f,g}$ on $B=\bigcup_oB_o$ such that $$\label{intro:moments}
[T_Df,g]=\int_B\chi_D\,d\nu_{f,g}
\quad\text{for every }D,$$ and that $$\label{intro:detector}
f\in\iota_*(\mathcal F_Y\mathcal A_E)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}(\nu_{f,g})\subseteq B_Y
\quad\text{for every }g\in\mathcal A_\mathbb C.$$ A finite complex measure means one of finite total variation. No positivity of these measures is asserted or needed.

The right side of <a href="#intro:detector" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="intro:detector">[intro:detector]</a> uses the same complex function space, point-counting Hecke operations, rational stack weights and compact sets for every $\iota$. It therefore gives invariance of the filtration under all coefficient automorphisms over $\mathbb Q$. Finite-coordinate linear algebra then proves <a href="#intro:main-map" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="intro:main-map">[intro:main-map]</a>.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### The local arguments

The supported trace concentration and injection statements in <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[GLR](#ref-GLR), Corollaries 2.1.10 and 2.1.13] are conditional on Conjecture 2.1.5 there, as stipulated in its Section 2.1.9. Section <a href="#tr:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="tr:section">5</a> proves the concentration and injection needed here from the component and orbit calculations, and constructs actual compact Weil representatives of the supported trace classes.

The central task is to detect every nonzero top support class by a finitely supported function test despite possible cancellation from its boundary. Our contribution is the bilinear criterion <a href="#intro:moments" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="intro:moments">[intro:moments]</a>–<a href="#intro:detector" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="intro:detector">[intro:detector]</a> on the full space of finitely supported functions, together with the local calculations and compact representatives that establish it. First, on a retained fixed component of the local-system stack, a pure Weil lift supplies separated Frobenius weights. Finite covariant tests recover the component from a closed-orbit slice while preserving completion along its invariant base. Nonresonance then gives a quadratic moment-map model compatible with evaluation bundles and singular support.

A deformation-theoretic antecedent is the quadratic-germ theorem of Goldman–Millson for compact Kähler manifolds and compact-image representations <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[GoldmanMillson](#ref-GoldmanMillson), Theorem 1]. Pridham obtains Frobenius-compatible classical deformation hulls and cup-product quadratic equations from purity for smooth proper varieties <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Pridham](#ref-Pridham), Theorem 2.11 and Corollary 2.12]. Our finite-jet nonresonance argument retains the derived equivariant model, its formal invariant base, evaluation bundles and singular-support map.

Second, a graded Koszul description reduces each nilpotent-orbit quotient to equivariant Clifford modules together with strictly contracting Hom directions. Clifford algebras and graded matrix factorizations also enter the spectral Whittaker construction of Ben-Zvi, Raskin and Venkatesh described in <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Raskin](#ref-Raskin), Section 4.5], which develops an idea of V. Lafforgue; the even-nilpotent construction appears in <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[BZSV](#ref-BZSV), Section 18.5]. Here a twisted-bar contraction proves trace concentration. Proper orbit-closure extensions and finite Koszul lifts produce actual compact Weil classes, so the result describes the image in <a href="#intro:filtration" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="intro:filtration">[intro:filtration]</a>, not only an abstract associated graded. The passage from equivariant localization to a cofiber sequence of traces follows the formalism of Hoyois–Scherotzke–Sibilla <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[HSS](#ref-HSS), Theorem 3.4 and Proposition 5.4].

Third, nonstandard automorphic duality turns these classes into the bilinear tests <a href="#intro:pairing" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="intro:pairing">[intro:pairing]</a>. Localization with supports gives decaying normal-direction contributions and hence finite complex measures. On a top orbit the common density is invertible; finite character approximations remove it and leave a nondegenerate coefficient pairing. A strict drop in ambient adjoint rank separates every proper boundary contribution. These two facts prevent cancellation from hiding a nonzero top class.

The supported local-cohomology and orbit-distribution strategy is inspired by Kazhdan–Okounkov’s treatment of the unramified Eisenstein spectrum <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[KazhdanOkounkov](#ref-KazhdanOkounkov), Sections 2.6.1–2.6.4 and 3.3–3.4]. The all-function bilinear detector, compact Weil lifts, and noncancellation and rational-descent arguments are developed here; that reference does not establish the rationality theorem above.

<figure id="intro:diagram">

<figcaption>Main line of the proof. The compact-set properties used by the measure criterion are proved independently in Section <a href="#desc:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="desc:section">7</a>.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-intro-diagram"></a>
**Figure source and visual witness** (`intro:diagram`)

Visual witness description of PDF page 5 (generated from the PDF page, not source text):

```text
A vertical flowchart diagram displaying four sequential stages connected by downward vertical arrows with transition labels:
1. Top stage: "quadratic component model (Section 3)"
   - Downward arrow labeled on the right: "orbit traces"
2. Second stage: "compact Weil trace classes (Sections 4–5)"
   - Downward arrow labeled on the right: "supported Hom pairings"
3. Third stage: "bilinear measure criterion (Section 6)"
   - Downward arrow labeled on the right: "coefficient descent"
4. Fourth stage: "rational form of $\mathcal{F}_Y \mathcal{A}_E$ (Section 7)"
```


Section <a href="#inp:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="inp:section">2</a> records the established geometric inputs and their precise scope. Sections <a href="#mod:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mod:section">3</a>–<a href="#tr:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="tr:section">5</a> prove the local model and trace calculations. Section <a href="#meas:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="meas:section">6</a> establishes the measure criterion, and Section <a href="#desc:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="desc:section">7</a> completes the rational descent.

<a id="inp:section"></a>
### Geometric inputs and Frobenius conventions

We use the constructible $\ell$-adic geometric Langlands framework. The results recalled here apply under Assumption <a href="#intro:characteristic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="intro:characteristic">1</a>: its first three conditions are those of <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[AGKRRV1](#ref-AGKRRV1), 14.4.1], and its fourth is the additional condition of <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[AGKRRV1](#ref-AGKRRV1), D.1.1]. All categories are $\overline{\mathbb Q}_{\ell}$-linear. A compact object means an object whose Hom functor commutes with filtered colimits; compactness is stronger than constructibility on an algebraic stack.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Restricted Langlands and functions

<a id="stmt-inp-langlands"></a>
<div id="inp:langlands" class="theorem">

**Theorem 3** (Restricted geometric Langlands). *There is a Frobenius-invariant union $Z$ of connected components of $\mathop{\mathrm{LocSys}}^{\mathrm{restr}}_{\widehat G}(\overline X)$ and an equivalence $$\mathbb L_G^{\mathrm{restr}}:\mathcal C
       \xrightarrow{\sim}\mathop{\mathrm{IndCoh}}_{\widehat{\mathcal N}}(Z).$$ It identifies $\mathcal C_Y$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{IndCoh}}_Y(Z)$ for every closed $\widehat G$-invariant $Y\subset\widehat{\mathcal N}$ and intertwines Hecke functors with tensoring by the corresponding evaluation bundles. If $f:Z\to Z$ sends a local system to its pullback along geometric Frobenius of $\overline X$, the functor corresponding to $\Phi=(\operatorname{Frob}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Bun}}})_*$ is $f^!$. The embedding $\mathcal C\hookrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Shv}}(\mathop{\mathrm{Bun}}_G(\overline X),\overline{\mathbb Q}_{\ell})$ preserves compact objects.*

</div>

The equivalence and the last assertion are <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[GR](#ref-GR), Theorems 1.3.9(i) and 1.1.7]; Frobenius invariance of the retained union is <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[GR](#ref-GR), 1.5.4]. The Hecke action is compatible with the spectral linear structure <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[GR](#ref-GR), Lemma 1.3.7], and the support compatibility is <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Raskin](#ref-Raskin), Theorem 4.1.1 and Corollary 4.2.1]. The stated Frobenius convention is exactly <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[GLR](#ref-GLR), 1.3.4]. Thus $Z$ is the prime union of the introduction throughout this paper. In particular, this theorem does not require identifying that union with the entire restricted stack. Under Assumption <a href="#intro:characteristic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="intro:characteristic">1</a>, the companion full-support theorem <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[RGLCompanion](#ref-RGLCompanion), Theorem 1.4, regime A] identifies $Z=\mathop{\mathrm{LocSys}}^{\mathrm{restr}}_{\widehat G}(\overline X)$ as spectral prestacks over the fixed coefficient field $E=\overline{\mathbb Q}_{\ell}$.

At a geometric point $x$, a representation $D$ of $\widehat G$ supplies the vector bundle $\sigma\mapsto D_{\sigma,x}$ on $Z$. At a closed point of degree $d$, the geometric points form a Frobenius cycle of length $d$. The corresponding Hecke construction uses all $d$ evaluation bundles, with cyclic transport. This observation will keep the degree of a closed point visible in our trace calculation.

<a id="stmt-inp-theta"></a>
<div id="inp:theta" class="remark">

*Remark 4* (Theta normalization). The theta normalization of the equivalence can be made over $\mathbf F_q$. Indeed, $X$ admits a line bundle $\vartheta$ defined over $\mathbf F_q$ with $\vartheta^{\otimes2}\simeq\omega_X$. For odd characteristic this follows from <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[Atiyah](#ref-Atiyah), 5, Remark 2 after Proposition 5.2, p. 61]: there is a Frobenius-invariant square-root class, and the Brauer group of the finite field vanishes, so this class descends. In characteristic two, choose a rational function $a\in\mathbf F_q(X)$ with $da\ne0$. The orders of $da$ are even, as a Laurent expansion over each perfect residue field shows. Hence $\mathcal O_X(\tfrac12\operatorname{div}(da))$ is such a square root <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[Mumford](#ref-Mumford), 4, p. 191]. No rational point of $X$ is needed.

</div>

<a id="stmt-inp-trace"></a>
<div id="inp:trace" class="theorem">

**Theorem 5** (Trace and ordinary local terms). *There is a canonical isomorphism of complexes $$\operatorname{LT}:\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\Phi,\mathcal C)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal A_{\overline{\mathbb Q}_{\ell}},$$ with the right-hand side in degree zero. For a compact object $M$ and a morphism $b:M\to\Phi M$, it sends the categorical class $[M,b]$ to the ordinary alternating trace function of the adjoint morphism $(\operatorname{Frob}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Bun}}})^*M\to M$. The function has finite support. This comparison is compatible with Hecke operations and with the sheaf–function operations of pullback, star tensor product, and compactly supported pushforward.*

</div>

The trace isomorphism is <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[AGKRRV3](#ref-AGKRRV3), Theorem 0.2.6, equivalently Corollary 4.1.4]. Its identification with local terms is <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[AGKRRV3](#ref-AGKRRV3), Theorem 0.6.8, equivalently Theorem 5.2.3, and Remark 0.6.5]; the compact-embedding hypothesis used there is provided by Theorem <a href="#inp:langlands" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="inp:langlands">3</a>. The compatibilities and the stack convention for summation are recalled in <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[GLR](#ref-GLR), 1.1]. This is a statement about all of $\mathcal A_{\overline{\mathbb Q}_{\ell}}$. It does not restrict to cuspidal or Hecke-finite functions. The stronger assertion that actual invertible Weil structures span the supported traces will be proved below.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Duality and finite evaluations

<a id="stmt-inp-duality"></a>
<div id="inp:duality" class="theorem">

<a id="inp:duality-pairing"></a>
**Theorem 6** (Nonstandard duality). *The pairing $$\label{inp:duality-pairing}
 \mathcal C\otimes\mathcal C\longrightarrow\operatorname{Vect}_{\overline{\mathbb Q}_{\ell}},\qquad
 (M,N)\longmapsto
 R\Gamma_c\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{Bun}}_G(\overline X),M\overset{*}{\otimes}N\bigr)$$ defines a self-duality of $\mathcal C$. It is invariant under simultaneous Frobenius. If $M,N$ are compact, its value is a perfect complex, also after any finite Hecke representation is inserted in either variable.*

</div>

The duality is <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[AGKRRV2](#ref-AGKRRV2), Theorem 3.2.2]. Here is the finiteness deduction, which is useful when applying it. By Theorem <a href="#inp:langlands" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="inp:langlands">3</a>, the two objects are compact in ambient sheaves, hence constructible <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[AGKRRV2](#ref-AGKRRV2), Appendix A.1.6]. Star tensoring an ambient compact with a constructible object preserves compactness <a id="pm-cite-32"></a>[[AGKRRV2](#ref-AGKRRV2), Appendix A.1.6]. For $p:\mathop{\mathrm{Bun}}_G\to\operatorname{pt}$, the functor $p_!$ is left adjoint to the continuous functor $p^!$ <a id="pm-cite-33"></a>[[AGKRRV2](#ref-AGKRRV2), Appendix A.1.7], so it preserves compactness. Its value is therefore compact in $\operatorname{Vect}_{\overline{\mathbb Q}_{\ell}}$, hence perfect. The Hecke functor for a finite-dimensional representation has a continuous adjoint supplied by its dual representation, and likewise preserves compactness. Frobenius invariance of <a href="#inp:duality-pairing" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="inp:duality-pairing">[inp:duality-pairing]</a> follows by change of variables and the compatibility of Frobenius with star tensor product.

<a id="inp:function-pairing"></a>
For compact Weil objects the numerical trace of <a href="#inp:duality-pairing" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="inp:duality-pairing">[inp:duality-pairing]</a> is consequently $$\label{inp:function-pairing}
 [u,g]=\sum_{b\in S}\frac{u(b)g(b)}{|\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(b)|}.$$ This is the Grothendieck trace formula with groupoid counting measure; see <a id="pm-cite-34"></a>[[GLR](#ref-GLR), 1.1.9 and 1.2.3]. The pairing is bilinear over $\overline{\mathbb Q}_{\ell}$, without complex conjugation. The compact objects of the categorical dual are the opposites of the compact objects of $\mathcal C$, and their evaluation is enriched Hom <a id="pm-cite-35"></a>[[AGKRRV2](#ref-AGKRRV2), 0.5.3 and 5.1.2]. Thus the second input may be transported to a compact Hom-test object. Its Weil arrow is reversed on passing to the opposite category. For arrows $a:\Phi M\to M$ and $b:N\to\Phi N$, the induced endomorphism of $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(M,N)$ is <a id="inp:hom-action"></a>$$\label{inp:hom-action}
 h\longmapsto\Phi^{-1}(b\circ h\circ a).$$ This convention fixes the direction of every Frobenius action below. The later spanning theorem will justify testing against every function in <a href="#inp:function-pairing" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="inp:function-pairing">[inp:function-pairing]</a>.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### The geometry of a spectral component

<a id="stmt-inp-components"></a>
<div id="inp:components" class="theorem">

<a id="inp:tangent"></a>
**Theorem 7** (Components and deformation theory). *Every connected component $Z_\sigma$ of the restricted local-system stack has a unique semisimple isomorphism class $\sigma$. Its automorphism group $J=\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(\sigma)$ is reductive, possibly disconnected. There is a formal affine coarse space $S_\sigma$ with reduced space a point, and $Z_\sigma\to S_\sigma$ is relatively algebraic. After choosing a frame at a geometric point, the component is formal affine. For an affine infinitesimal stage $S_n\to S_\sigma$, its base change is affine; almost finite type stages and bounded Postnikov truncations may be used. These affine stages have coherent cohomology in each bounded range. The tangent complex at a local system $\tau$ is $$\label{inp:tangent}
 T_\tau\mathop{\mathrm{LocSys}}^{\mathrm{restr}}_{\widehat G}(\overline X)
       \simeq R\Gamma(\overline X,\widehat{\mathfrak g}_\tau)[1].$$ In particular the stack is quasi-smooth.*

</div>

The component and finiteness statements assemble <a id="pm-cite-36"></a>[[AGKRRV1](#ref-AGKRRV1), Theorems 1.4.5 and 1.6.3, Remarks 1.4.6–1.4.7, Corollary 3.7.4, and Theorem 5.4.2]. The affine fiber assertion is Corollary 5.4.6 there; the assertion for general affine infinitesimal stages is made explicitly in <a id="pm-cite-37"></a>[[AGKRRV1](#ref-AGKRRV1), 7.9.10]. The deformation formula is <a id="pm-cite-38"></a>[[AGKRRV1](#ref-AGKRRV1), Proposition 2.2.2 and 2.4, 25.4.3]. Formal quasi-smoothness is established in <a id="pm-cite-39"></a>[[AGKRRV1](#ref-AGKRRV1), 21.2.1–21.2.3]; the deformation complex has the required amplitude because the curve has cohomological dimension two. Semisimplicity of $\sigma$ makes its geometric monodromy reductive, and its centralizer $J$ is reductive in characteristic zero.

For $d$ frames, write $K=\widehat G^d$. Adding the remaining frames is an affine torsor, so the same finite-stage assertions hold. The unique closed $K$-orbit is $K/J$: closed orbits correspond to semisimple local systems, and the stabilizer of a framed representative is $J$. Every nonempty invariant closed subset of a finite stage meets this orbit. These statements supply the finite-stage setting for the reconstruction in the next section; they do not yet identify the component with a quadratic moment-map model.

<a id="stmt-inp-pure-lift"></a>
<div id="inp:pure-lift" class="theorem">

**Theorem 8** (Pure Weil lift). *Fix an abstract field isomorphism $\iota:\overline{\mathbb Q}_{\ell}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb C$. If $Z_\sigma$ is Frobenius invariant, its semisimple point $\sigma$ admits a Weil structure that is $\iota$-pure of weight zero. In particular, writing $\widehat{\mathfrak g}_\sigma$ for its adjoint local system, every Frobenius eigenvalue on $H^i(\overline X,\widehat{\mathfrak g}_\sigma)$ has $\iota$-absolute value $q^{i/2}$, for $0\leq i\leq2$.*

</div>

The pure-lift application to a Frobenius-fixed semisimple point is stated in <a id="pm-cite-40"></a>[[GLR](#ref-GLR), Appendix A.5.4–A.5.5], using <a id="pm-cite-41"></a>[[AGKRRV1](#ref-AGKRRV1), Corollary 3.7.4 and Proposition 25.4.6]. The literal statement of that proposition treats irreducible Weil parameters; the cited appendix records the semisimple consequence used here. Purity of $H^1$ is the smooth proper curve application of Weil II <a id="pm-cite-42"></a>[[Deligne](#ref-Deligne), Corollary 3.3.6 and 3.3.10], also stated explicitly in <a id="pm-cite-43"></a>[[GLR](#ref-GLR), Appendix A.5.5]. For $H^0$ it follows by viewing horizontal sections in a pure fiber, and for $H^2$ by curve duality. No semisimplicity of the cohomological Frobenius operators is asserted or required.

Put $\mathfrak j=\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(J)$ and $V=H^1(\overline X,\widehat{\mathfrak g}_\sigma)$. An invariant form on $\widehat{\mathfrak g}$ and curve duality give $$H^0(\overline X,\widehat{\mathfrak g}_\sigma)=\mathfrak j,
 \qquad H^2(\overline X,\widehat{\mathfrak g}_\sigma)\simeq\mathfrak j^*(-1),$$ and a $J$-invariant symplectic form $V\otimes V\to\overline{\mathbb Q}_{\ell}(-1)$. Its moment map is the cup–bracket obstruction. After forgetting the Tate factor it is the usual quadratic map $\mu:V\to\mathfrak j^*$. We will use purity to linearize Frobenius on the full formal component, including this obstruction and the evaluation bundles.

<a id="mod:section"></a>
### A quadratic model with its Frobenius action

We now replace each Frobenius-fixed retained component by a derived quadratic moment-map model, retaining its evaluation bundles and the map defining nilpotent singular support. The main issue is to extend coordinates constructed at its closed semisimple orbit over the formal invariant base.

Fix an abstract field isomorphism $\overline{\mathbb Q}_{\ell}\simeq\mathbb C$. All absolute values in this section refer to this isomorphism. An invertible linear operator has *absolute weight $w$* if all its eigenvalues have modulus $q^{w/2}$. This terminology does not assert that the operator is semisimple. It is distinct from both cohomological degree and the weights of an $\mathfrak{sl}_2$-triple.

The spectral stack remains the prime union $Z$ from Section <a href="#inp:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="inp:section">2</a>. Its components form a categorical coproduct. In the twisted bar construction, an arrow string closes only in a component preserved by $\Phi$. Consequently <a id="mod:component-trace"></a>$$\label{mod:component-trace}
 \mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\Phi,\mathop{\mathrm{IndCoh}}_Y(Z))
 \simeq\bigoplus_{Z_\sigma\text{ preserved by }\Phi}
       \mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\Phi,\mathop{\mathrm{IndCoh}}_Y(Z_\sigma)).$$ The sum is a direct sum, including when there are infinitely many components. There are no arrows between distinct components; this also proves their orthogonality for the Hom pairings used below.

<a id="mod:moment"></a>
Choose such a component and its unique closed semisimple parameter $\sigma$. Choose a pure Weil lift as in Theorem <a href="#inp:pure-lift" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="inp:pure-lift">8</a>, and put $$J=\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(\sigma),\qquad \mathfrak j=\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(J),\qquad
 V=H^1(X_{\overline{\mathbb F}_q},\widehat{\mathfrak g}_\sigma).$$ The group $J$ is reductive; it need not be connected. Curve duality and an invariant form identify $H^2(\widehat{\mathfrak g}_\sigma)$ with $\mathfrak j^*(-1)$ and give a $J$-invariant symplectic form $\omega$ on $V$, after forgetting its Tate factor. The moment map is $$\label{mod:moment}
 \mu:V\longrightarrow\mathfrak j^*,\qquad
 \langle\mu(x),z\rangle=\tfrac12\omega(zx,x).$$ Its zero fiber will always mean its *derived* zero fiber. In particular no regular-sequence assumption on the components of $\mu$ is being made.

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
#### What is recovered from completion at the closed orbit

We first explain why constructing a model near $\sigma$ suffices here. The invariant base must be retained in this argument. Choose a closed point $v$ of degree $d$ and frames at its $d$ geometric points. Write $K=\widehat G^d$. The framed component supplied by Theorem <a href="#inp:components" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="inp:components">7</a> is an ind-affine derived scheme over a formal invariant base with reduced scheme a point. Its unique closed $K$-orbit is $K/J$. It has affine almost-finite-type stages over nilpotent thickenings of that invariant point. At each such stage the classical coordinate ring is noetherian, its invariant ring is Artinian, and its cohomology sheaves in any bounded Postnikov range are coherent. Adding the other frames to a one-frame presentation is an affine operation. These are precisely the finite-stage properties we use.

<a id="stmt-mod-finite-covariants"></a>
<div id="mod:finite-covariants" class="lemma">

**Lemma 9** (Finite covariants and completion). *Let $K$ be a reductive complex algebraic group, possibly disconnected, and let $A$ be a finitely generated $K$-algebra. Suppose $A^K$ is Artinian and $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}A$ has a unique closed $K$-orbit $O$, with ideal $I$. For an equivariant finite $A$-module $M$ and a finite-dimensional $K$-representation $D$, the inverse system $$\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_K(D,M/I^nM)$$ is eventually constant with value $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_K(D,M)$. In particular the rational, locally $K$-finite part of the $I$-adic completion recovers $M$, and recovers $A$ together with its multiplication when $M=A$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-mod-finite-covariants"><summary>Proof of stmt-mod-finite-covariants</summary>

*Proof.* Choose a finite-dimensional $K$-stable generating space $U\subset M$. The surjection $A\otimes U\to M$ induces a surjection on $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_K(D,-)$, by reductivity. Finite generation of the covariants of $A$ over $A^K$ <a id="pm-cite-44"></a>[[Brion](#ref-Brion), Lemma 2.3] therefore makes $M_D=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_K(D,M)$ finite over $A^K$, hence finite-dimensional. This applies to disconnected $K$: its identity component is reductive and its component group is finite. Let $N=\bigcap_{n\geq1}I^nM$. It is a finite equivariant submodule because $A$ is noetherian. At every point of $O$ its localization vanishes by Krull intersection <a id="pm-cite-45"></a>[[Stacks](#ref-Stacks), Tag 00IP]. If $N$ were nonzero, its nonempty invariant closed support would contain a closed orbit, hence would meet $O$. Therefore $N=0$.

The descending subspaces $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_K(D,I^nM)$ of $M_D$ have zero intersection and thus are eventually zero. Reductivity in characteristic zero makes $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_K(D,-)$ exact, so $$\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_K(D,M/I^nM)
 =M_D/\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_K(D,I^nM).$$ This proves the assertion. Every rational representation is the union of its finite-dimensional subrepresentations; applying the assertion to all $D$ therefore recovers $M$. Applied also to finite tensor products of representation spaces, it recovers multiplication and every equivariant finite-presentation map. ◻

</details>

For example, let $\mathbb G_m$ act with weights $1,-1$ on the coordinate functions $x,y$, and put $$A_N=\mathbb C[x,y]/((xy)^N),\qquad
 A_N^{\mathbb G_m}=\mathbb C[t]/(t^N),\quad t=xy,\quad N\ge1.$$ The origin is the unique closed orbit. Each weight space of $A_N$ is finite-dimensional, so the locally $\mathbb G_m$-finite part of its $(x,y)$-adic completion recovers $A_N$. The ordinary inverse limit also contains arbitrary formal series such as $\sum_{m\ge0}x^m$, which are not locally finite. Thus rational recovery retains the nonzero points on both axes, not only the closed orbit. As $N$ varies, these stages give the formal neighborhood of $xy=0$, including the formal invariant parameter $t$. The next lemma carries out this recovery for derived algebras and their maps.

<a id="stmt-mod-reconstruction"></a>
<div id="mod:reconstruction" class="lemma">

**Lemma 10** (Restoring the invariant base). *For the framed component above, an equivariant isomorphism of its completion along $K/J$ with another such completed model extends uniquely over the formal invariant bases to an isomorphism of their invariant-fiber completions. The same assertion holds for equivariant maps and their full mapping spaces, and for finite equivariant presentation data. Here the extension is unique up to a contractible space of choices.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-mod-reconstruction"><summary>Proof of stmt-mod-reconstruction</summary>

*Proof.* We give the derived argument, since recovering coordinate rings alone would not recover homotopies. The proof has three stages: finite covariants recover each affine stage and its mapping spaces; trivial covariants recover the invariant base; derived Koszul thickenings then give compatible extensions over that base. Write $\mathcal R_K$ for the derived category of rational $K$-representations. All limits in this paragraph are taken in $\mathcal R_K$, not in the category of vector spaces with an arbitrary, possibly nonrational, $K$-action.

*Recovery at a fixed invariant-base stage.* Let $A$ be its connective coordinate algebra, viewed as a commutative algebra in $\mathcal R_K$. The precise hypotheses are that $H^0(A)$ is finitely generated, $H^0(A)^K$ is Artinian, $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}H^0(A)$ has a unique closed orbit $O$, and every $H^i(A)$ is finite over $H^0(A)$. Choose a finite-dimensional $K$-stable generating space for the ideal of $O$ in $H^0(A)$, lift its generators to degree-zero cycles, and extend them to a finite-dimensional stable generating space for $H^0(A)$. Reductivity allows these lifts to be equivariant. This gives a map from a polynomial $K$-algebra $P$ to $A$, surjective on $H^0$. Let $I_P$ be the ideal generated by the chosen orbit equations and put $$A_n=A\otimes_P^{\mathbf L}P/I_P^n.$$ These derived orbit jets compute completion along $O$; the module-level comparison is <a id="pm-cite-46"></a>[[Stacks](#ref-Stacks), Tag 0BKH]. The resulting formal neighborhood is independent of the generating presentation <a id="pm-cite-47"></a>[[AGKRRV1](#ref-AGKRRV1), Theorem A.1.3(c) and A.1.5].

<a id="mod:derived-covariants"></a>
<a id="mod:mapping-spaces"></a>
Here is the cohomological calculation at these jets. Every $H^i(A)$ is a finite $P$-module. Noetherian Artin–Rees makes the positive-Tor systems $\{\operatorname{Tor}^P_j(H^i(A),P/I_P^n)\}_n$, $j>0$, pro-zero. Since $P$ has finite global dimension, the hyper-Tor calculation has finite width in every cohomological degree, even if $A$ is unbounded below. Consequently $$\{H^i(A_n)\}_n\simeq
 \{H^i(A)/I_P^nH^i(A)\}_n$$ as pro-modules. This is the coherent derived-completion calculation of <a id="pm-cite-48"></a>[[Stacks](#ref-Stacks), Tags 00MA, 0A06, and 0BKH], applied here over the ordinary noetherian polynomial ring $P$. For every finite-dimensional $K$-representation $D$, Lemma <a href="#mod:finite-covariants" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mod:finite-covariants">9</a> and exactness of covariants therefore give a natural pro-isomorphism $$\label{mod:derived-covariants}
 \{H^i\mathop{\mathrm{RHom}}_K(D,A_n)\}_n
 \simeq H^i\mathop{\mathrm{RHom}}_K(D,A),$$ with a constant system on the right. In particular these systems have zero $\lim^1$. The functors $\mathop{\mathrm{RHom}}_K(D,-)$ commute with limits and jointly detect equivalences in $\mathcal R_K$. Applying the Milnor exact sequence to <a href="#mod:derived-covariants" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mod:derived-covariants">[mod:derived-covariants]</a> proves <a id="mod:rational-derived-completion"></a>$$\label{mod:rational-derived-completion}
 A\xrightarrow{\sim}\operatorname*{holim}_n A_n
       \quad\text{in }\operatorname{CAlg}(\mathcal R_K).$$ Indeed the forgetful functor from commutative algebras creates limits and detects equivalences. Thus this is a natural equivalence of algebras, not merely an identification of their cohomology. It gives, for every rational equivariant coordinate algebra $B$, an equivalence of full mapping spaces $$\label{mod:mapping-spaces}
 \operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{CAlg}(\mathcal R_K)}(B,A)
 \simeq
 \operatorname*{holim}_n
 \operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{CAlg}(\mathcal R_K)}(B,A_n),$$ compatible with composition. No index $n$ uniform in $i$ or $D$ has been asserted or used. The rational limit is essential: as in the preceding example, the ordinary vector-space limit would retain arbitrary formal series. The same calculation applies to an $A$-module with coherent cohomology. Finite equivariant module presentations and their maps are recovered by testing their generating representation spaces; their relations and coherent homotopies are recovered by the corresponding derived mapping spaces.

*Recovery of the invariant base.* The formal coarse base is constructed from the system of trivial covariants of the framed affine stages <a id="pm-cite-49"></a>[[AGKRRV1](#ref-AGKRRV1), 5.3.5–5.3.6]. We do not identify an arbitrarily chosen base stage with the spectrum of its pullback’s invariant algebra. The trivial case of <a href="#mod:derived-covariants" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mod:derived-covariants">[mod:derived-covariants]</a> instead recovers this entire system. On classical invariant functions the topologies are cofinal: if $\mathfrak m$ is the invariant ideal of definition, then $\mathfrak m\subset I$, and, after passage to a fixed nilpotent invariant-base stage, Lemma <a href="#mod:finite-covariants" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mod:finite-covariants">9</a> makes the trivial covariants of high powers of $I$ vanish. Applying this in every bounded range gives the corresponding comparison for the derived invariant systems. An equivariant map of orbit completions therefore gives the formal base map as well.

<a id="mod:koszul-base-stages"></a>
*Factorization and extension.* The noetherian formal-affine presentation of that base uses finitely many invariant generators $t_1,\ldots,t_r$ of an ideal of definition. The noetherian and coherent presentation is supplied by <a id="pm-cite-50"></a>[[AGKRRV1](#ref-AGKRRV1), Theorem A.1.3(b),(c) and Remark A.1.4]. Its infinitesimal stages may be taken to be the derived Koszul thickenings $$\label{mod:koszul-base-stages}
 R_N=R/\!/(t_1^N,\ldots,t_r^N)
   :=R\otimes_{\mathbb C[u_1,\ldots,u_r]}^{\mathbf L}\mathbb C,
 \qquad u_j\longmapsto t_j^N.$$ These are precisely the formal-affine stages used in <a id="pm-cite-51"></a>[[AGKRRV1](#ref-AGKRRV1), 7.1 and 7.9.8–7.9.10]. The relevant framed base changes are affine and almost of finite type.

Restrict a map of orbit completions to the completed orbit of one source stage $A$. Compose it with the target’s map to its formal base and then with the structural map to $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}R$. Equation <a href="#mod:mapping-spaces" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mod:mapping-spaces">[mod:mapping-spaces]</a>, applied to the base algebra $R$ with trivial $K$-action, recovers a map $R\to A$ and its homotopies. The images of the $t_j$ vanish at the reduced invariant point, so they are nilpotent in the Artinian algebra $H^0(A)^K$. Choose a common power $N$ and invariant nullhomotopies of these $t_j^N$. Such nullhomotopies exist because vanishing in $H^0$ means being a boundary; they may be chosen invariant by reductivity. The universal property of <a href="#mod:koszul-base-stages" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mod:koszul-base-stages">[mod:koszul-base-stages]</a> now factors this base map through a derived Koszul stage. Pulling back the target framed component along that stage factors the completed-space map through an affine target stage. Nilpotence alone would not justify factorization through an ordinary quotient. If $B_N$ is the coordinate algebra of the resulting affine target stage, the completed-stage map is a compatible point of the right-hand side of <a href="#mod:mapping-spaces" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mod:mapping-spaces">[mod:mapping-spaces]</a>, with $B=B_N$. It therefore extends to a map on the source stage $A$, with all its homotopies and composition data.

For clarity, the choices of powers and nullhomotopies do not add extra maps. The Koszul stages are cofinal tests for a formal neighborhood. One can check this directly on mapping spaces: above a fixed map with nilpotent $t_j$, the choices of nullhomotopy are torsors for the corresponding additive loop spaces, and increasing $N$ to $M$ acts on their differences by multiplication by $t_j^{M-N}$. Nilpotence kills every such homotopy group after passing far enough in this filtered system. Its nonempty space of choices is therefore contractible. This verifies cofinality for maps and homotopies, without interchanging a filtered colimit with a Postnikov limit. Equation <a href="#mod:mapping-spaces" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mod:mapping-spaces">[mod:mapping-spaces]</a> makes the extended maps on different source stages compatible. Applying it also to inverse maps and to their compositions completes the proof. ◻

</details>

Thus completion along the closed orbit is used to *construct* coordinates. It is not substituted for completion along the invariant null fiber. For example, nonclosed points of that null fiber are retained by Lemma <a href="#mod:reconstruction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mod:reconstruction">10</a>.

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
#### Simultaneous linearization

Here is the elementary nonresonance calculation that removes the higher relations in the local deformation problem. The classical Frobenius-compatible quadratic hull in the smooth proper, pure weight-zero setting is established in <a id="pm-cite-52"></a>[[Pridham](#ref-Pridham), Theorem 2.11 and Corollary 2.12]; its proof also uses Jordan decomposition on finite jets. We retain the derived presentation and equivariant data, then apply Lemma <a href="#mod:reconstruction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mod:reconstruction">10</a> to restore the formal invariant base.

<a id="stmt-mod-nonresonance"></a>
<div id="mod:nonresonance" class="lemma">

**Lemma 11** (Nonresonance with Jordan blocks). *Let a minimal complete free graded-commutative algebra have generators $V^*$ in cohomological degree $0$ and $R^*$ in degree $-1$. Suppose a continuous graded automorphism has linear parts of absolute weights $-1$ and $-2$ on these two generator spaces. It is conjugate, by a formal graded coordinate change tangent to the identity, to its linear part. If it commutes with a minimal differential, that differential, in the resulting coordinates, sends $R^*$ to quadratic polynomials in $V^*$. These assertions hold equivariantly for a reductive group normalized by the automorphism.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-mod-nonresonance"><summary>Proof of stmt-mod-nonresonance</summary>

*Proof.* Filter coordinate changes by total generator order. At order $n\geq2$ the error in a degree-zero generator belongs to $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(V^*,\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^nV^*)$. All eigenvalues of the conjugacy operator “linear target action times inverse linear source action” on this space have modulus $q^{-(n-1)/2}$, hence differ from $1$. An error in a degree-minus-one generator contains exactly one degree-minus-one factor and $n-1$ degree-zero factors. The corresponding operator on $$\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(R^*,R^*\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^{n-1}V^*)$$ has the same modulus $q^{-(n-1)/2}$. In either case its difference from the identity is invertible. This assertion follows from its eigenvalues and holds on generalized eigenspaces; it requires no diagonalization. Solve the conjugacy equation at this order and continue. The corrections converge in the generator-adic topology.

In the equivariant case the errors and corrections lie in the equivariant subspaces of these finite-dimensional jet spaces. The conjugacy operator preserves these subspaces and its inverse does also, so the same construction is equivariant. Transport the differential along the coordinate changes. The differential of a degree-zero generator is zero by degree. That of a degree-minus-one generator has no constant or linear term by minimality. Since the differential commutes with the now linear automorphism, an order-$n$ term can occur only if its weight $-n$ agrees with $-2$. Thus only $n=2$ remains. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-mod-quadratic-model"></a>
<div id="mod:quadratic-model" class="theorem">

<a id="mod:quadratic-equation"></a>
**Theorem 12** (The framed quadratic model). *<span id="mod:model" label="mod:model"></span> The retained component $Z_\sigma$, with its Frobenius action, evaluation bundles at the chosen cyclic tuple, and singular-support map, admits the model $$\label{mod:quadratic-equation}
 Z_\sigma\simeq
 \bigl[(\mu^{-1}(0))^{\wedge}_{\mathrm{unst}}/J\bigr].$$ Here $\mathrm{unst}$ is the inverse image of the origin under $V\to V/\!/J$, and completion is along this invariant null fiber. In this model:*

1.  *the singularity scheme has points $(x,z)$ satisfying $\mu(x)=0$ and $zx=0$, with $z\in\mathfrak j$;*

2.  *the support condition defined by $Y$ is the condition that the image of $z$ under $\mathfrak j\hookrightarrow\widehat{\mathfrak g}$ belongs to $Y$;*

3.  *every evaluation representation of $K=\widehat G^d$ is the bundle associated to its restriction along $J\hookrightarrow K$; Frobenius has constant cyclic transport on these bundles;*

4.  *after removing the scalars $q^{1/2}$ on deformation points and $q^{-1}$ on singular points, Frobenius is a linear automorphism of the moment-map data, normalizing $J$ and the framed representation labels. Its eigenvalues on these normalized linear data have modulus one.*

*The meaning of Frobenius on Hom complexes in <span class="upright">(iv)</span> is fixed explicitly in Lemma <a href="#mod:hom-action" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mod:hom-action">13</a> below.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-mod-quadratic-model"><summary>Proof of stmt-mod-quadratic-model</summary>

*Proof.* We first work in the completion of the framed component along $K/J$. The smooth affine orbit admits an equivariant formal retraction: lift the identity retraction through successive nilpotent neighborhoods, using smoothness, and average the lifting torsors using reductivity. The fiber is a formal slice with stabilizer $J$; the completed framed space is its induction from $J$ to $K$. Equivalently, this follows by splitting the orbit tangent directions and comparing tangent complexes at every lifting step.

The transverse tangent and obstruction spaces are respectively $V=H^1(\widehat{\mathfrak g}_\sigma)$ and $H^2(\widehat{\mathfrak g}_\sigma)$. Quasi-smoothness therefore gives a minimal presentation by complete free coordinates dual to these spaces in degrees $0$ and $-1$. One obtains this presentation from a free resolution by removing the contractible linear pairs; the splittings can be chosen $J$-equivariantly.

We recall the second-order obstruction calculation to fix its coefficient and sign, working over $E$ before the chosen coefficient transport. Use the simplicial descent model for local systems underlying <a href="#inp:tangent" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="inp:tangent">[inp:tangent]</a>, computed by hypercover cochains with the $\ell$-adic limit retained. Its adjoint cochains compute $R\Gamma(\overline X,\widehat{\mathfrak g}_\sigma)$, and its cup bracket combines the cup product with the Lie bracket of the adjoint coefficients. For a formal parameter $t$, write a perturbation of the descent data modulo $t^3$ as $g_{ij}\exp(t x_{ij}+t^2 y_{ij})$, with the cochains twisted by the original transition data $g_{ij}$. Expanding the cocycle equation by the Baker–Campbell–Hausdorff formula gives $$dx=0,\qquad dy=-\tfrac12[x,x].$$ Thus the quadratic obstruction is the class of $\tfrac12[x,x]$ in $H^2(\overline X,\widehat{\mathfrak g}_\sigma)$. This calculation uses a cochain model computing the deformation problem, rather than an assumed finite trivializing cover. Invariance of the coefficient pairing and graded commutativity give, with the duality and sign convention of <a href="#mod:moment" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mod:moment">[mod:moment]</a>, $$\bigl\langle\tfrac12[x,x],z\bigr\rangle
       =\tfrac12\omega(zx,x)=\langle\mu(x),z\rangle.$$ This identifies the quadratic obstruction with $\mu(x)$.

We next arrange that both the retraction and these coordinates respect the chosen Frobenius transporter. Purity gives absolute point weights $0,1,2$ on the orbit tangent, transverse tangent, and obstruction spaces. A positive-transverse-order error in the retraction has positive source weight and orbit target weight zero. Its Frobenius conjugacy operator therefore has no eigenvalue $1$. At every finite jet order it can be removed uniquely by solving the same linear equation used in Lemma <a href="#mod:nonresonance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mod:nonresonance">11</a>. The errors are equivariant sections of the tangent bundle of the smooth orbit; they are determined by their $J$-equivariant values at its base point. The stabilizer transport and the cyclic permutation of frames have weight zero. This constructs a Frobenius-compatible retraction and an invariant slice.

Represent its single cyclic automorphism on the minimal free presentation. Such a representative can be chosen equivariantly by free lifting and reductivity. Its invertible linear part makes it an automorphism of the complete graded algebra. There is no additional relation to impose on the generator of an infinite cyclic action. On formal coordinate functions use inverse transport; its generator weights are $-1,-2$. Lemma <a href="#mod:nonresonance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mod:nonresonance">11</a> makes this automorphism linear and forces the transported differential to be quadratic. A coordinate change tangent to the identity preserves that quadratic part, so the differential is precisely $\mu$. This proves the moment-map presentation on the orbit completion. Lemma <a href="#mod:reconstruction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mod:reconstruction">10</a> extends it over the invariant base, giving <a href="#mod:quadratic-equation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mod:quadratic-equation">[mod:quadratic-equation]</a>.

For its singularities one computes directly that $$\langle d\mu_x(v),z\rangle=\omega(zx,v).$$ Nondegeneracy of $\omega$ identifies $\ker(d\mu_x^*)$ with $\{z:zx=0\}$, proving <span class="upright">(i)</span>. The singular evaluation map is fiberwise linear in $z$. At $x=0$ curve duality identifies it with the centralizer inclusion $\mathfrak j\hookrightarrow\widehat{\mathfrak g}$, with its duality Tate factor. The target singular points have absolute weight $-2$. A correction of positive degree $n$ in $x$, linear in $z$, would have weight $n-2$, and cannot intertwine this Frobenius action. Comparison on each finite jet, again valid for Jordan blocks, excludes every such term. This proves <span class="upright">(ii)</span>. Changing the invariant form only rescales the simple factors of $\widehat{\mathfrak g}$, which preserves every nilpotent orbit.

Finally, a $K$-frame is exactly the datum trivializing all the evaluation bundles in question. Write $\mathscr S$ for the formal slice. The equivariant retraction identifies the framed completion with $K\times^J\mathscr S$, with its map to $K/J$ given by $[k,s]\mapsto kJ$. For a representation $D$ of $K$, quotienting the trivial framed bundle by $K$ therefore gives the bundle on $[\mathscr S/J]$ associated to $D|_J$. The retraction is Frobenius-compatible, so the transport on its $K/J$ coordinate is the closed-orbit transport, independent of $s$. The same is therefore true of the cyclic transport on every evaluation bundle. Purity on the chosen Weil parameter gives modulus-one eigenvalues on every normalized frame representation. A cyclic action can be tested on its $d$th power. Together with purity on $H^1$ and $H^2$, this proves <span class="upright">(iii)</span> and <span class="upright">(iv)</span>. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-13"></a>
#### Which action contracts Hom complexes

The next convention is essential: the category still has the endomorphism $\Phi$ specified in the problem.

<a id="stmt-mod-hom-action"></a>
<div id="mod:hom-action" class="lemma">

<a id="mod:T"></a>
<a id="mod:S"></a>
**Lemma 13** (Inverse transport on arrows). *<span id="mod:frobenius" label="mod:frobenius"></span> Choose structures $\alpha_M:\Phi M\xrightarrow{\sim}M$ on compact objects. On an arrow $h:M\to N$ put $$\begin{aligned}
 T(h)&=\alpha_N\,\Phi(h)\,\alpha_M^{-1},\label{mod:T}\\
 S(h)&=\Phi^{-1}(\alpha_N^{-1}h\alpha_M)=T^{-1}(h).
 \label{mod:S}
\end{aligned}$$ The action in the bilinear Hom pairing is $S$. On the quadratic model it gives absolute weight $-1$ to deformation functions and absolute weight $+2$ to degree-two obstruction operators. Thus its associated point weights on $(x,z)$ are $(1,-2)$, and $W(x,z)=\langle z,\mu(x)\rangle$ has weight zero.*

<a id="mod:inverse-homotopy"></a>
*The graph identification in <a href="#tr:graph-identification" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="tr:graph-identification">[tr:graph-identification]</a> turns the original $\Phi$-graph bimodule into the graph of the transported arrow action. In those coordinates the induced simultaneous bar actions $T$ and $S$ are inverse. If the paracyclic homotopy is $bH+Hb=1-T$, then $$\label{mod:inverse-homotopy}
 b(-T^{-1}H)+(-T^{-1}H)b=1-S.$$ In particular using contracting $S$-weights does not replace $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\Phi,-)$ by $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\Phi^{-1},-)$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-mod-hom-action"><summary>Proof of stmt-mod-hom-action</summary>

*Proof.* The Frobenius compatibility in Section <a href="#inp:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="inp:section">2</a> identifies $\Phi$ with $f^!$, where $f$ is pullback of parameters by geometric Frobenius of the curve. Since $f$ is an automorphism, this is its usual pullback functor. A covariant trace structure is $b:N\to\Phi N$, whereas a structure on the opposite input is $a:\Phi M\to M$. Their pairing takes an arrow to $$h\longmapsto\Phi^{-1}(b\,h\,a).$$ With $b=\alpha_N^{-1}$ and $a=\alpha_M$ this is <a href="#mod:S" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mod:S">[mod:S]</a>. It is the inverse of <a href="#mod:T" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mod:T">[mod:T]</a>, directly by composition.

For the signs, consider a coordinate and a relation generator with $f^\#x=a_1x$ and $f^\#\epsilon=a_2\epsilon$, where $|x|=0$, $|\epsilon|=-1$. Inverse transport on the endomorphisms of the free module sends multiplication by $x$ to multiplication by $a_1^{-1}x$. In a Koszul resolution the degree-two operator dual to $\epsilon$ transforms by $a_2$ under the same transport. Thus purity, $|a_1|=q^{1/2}$ and $|a_2|=q$, gives the stated signs. The calculation is linear-algebraic and applies to generalized eigenspaces. The polynomial operator dual to $\epsilon$ is a linear function on the singular variable $z$; point weights are the contragredient weights, giving $-2$ on $z$.

There is a distinction between ordinary arrows and raw graph coefficients. In the left-graph convention for classes $M\to\Phi M$, a raw coefficient is $m\in\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(M,\Phi N)$. It is identified with an ordinary arrow by $m\mapsto\alpha_Nm$. The simultaneous operator on this raw coefficient is $$m\longmapsto\Phi(\alpha_N)\Phi(m)\alpha_M^{-1},$$ not the ordinary-arrow formula <a href="#mod:T" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mod:T">[mod:T]</a>. Under the stated graph identification it becomes the transported ordinary-arrow action. The full multiobject identification, including permuted labels and the last twisted bar face, is proved in Lemma <a href="#tr:bar-contraction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="tr:bar-contraction">21</a>; see <a href="#tr:graph-identification" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="tr:graph-identification">[tr:graph-identification]</a>–<a href="#tr:graph-transport" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="tr:graph-transport">[tr:graph-transport]</a>. Thus the paracyclic homotopy is transported through an actual isomorphism of graph bimodules. In these coordinates $T$ commutes with $b$, and multiplying $bH+Hb=1-T$ by $-T^{-1}$ proves <a href="#mod:inverse-homotopy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mod:inverse-homotopy">[mod:inverse-homotopy]</a>. For permuted labels a common positive power is used only to read their weights. The graph throughout is canonically identified with the original $\Phi$-graph; neither a power of $\Phi$ nor $\Phi^{-1}$ is substituted for it. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-14"></a>
#### Compact normalization, including the frame labels

For the measure calculation, modulus-one eigenvalues before twisting are not enough: they must remain of modulus one after every compact stabilizer twist. We establish a simultaneous normalization of the deformation and frame data. Choose a faithful representation $R$ of $\widehat G$ and include in the linear data the direct sum of its fibers at all $d$ framed points. The $d$th power of the cyclic transporter acts on these summands by the pure weight-zero closed-point Weil transports. Hence the cyclic transporter itself has modulus-one eigenvalues.
