The same geometric bound is summable after raising each eigenvalue modulus to any positive power, in particular $1/t$. It therefore gives summability also for $S$, whose one-step action has zero trace on the resulting permuted sum. On fixed strata the preceding calculation applies directly. The full scalar evaluation is perfect by Lemma <a href="#meas:hom-functions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="meas:hom-functions">25</a>, so its convergent alternating character is its ordinary finite supertrace. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-27"></a>
#### Constructing the measures

Let $L_{e,c}$ be the maximal compact subgroup of $L_e$ chosen in Lemma <a href="#mod:compact-normalization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mod:compact-normalization">14</a>, including all components. Denote by $\tau_e$ the triple-adjusted $S$-action, using that normalized transporter and retaining the absolute-weight scalars in the displayed table above. On coefficient modules in lifted degree zero it is the normalized transporter itself. Expressions on the corresponding compact coset are written as functions of $k\in L_{e,c}$, with operator $k\tau_e$ on the space in question. Haar measure $dk$ is probability measure on the entire compact group. This notation allows $\tau_e$ to act nontrivially on $L_e$.

<a id="meas:compact-projection"></a>
Projection to reductive invariants is integration over $L_{e,c}$: $$\label{meas:compact-projection}
 \operatorname{str}(\tau_e;H^{L_e})
 =\int_{L_{e,c}}\operatorname{str}(k\tau_e;H)\,dk.$$ For a finite-dimensional representation this is the Reynolds projection, which commutes with $\tau_e$ because it normalizes the compact group. Lemma <a href="#meas:supported-convergence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="meas:supported-convergence">26</a> proves the formula for all the convergent characters here by uniform absolute convergence. It also applies when $L_e$ is disconnected: averaging over its finitely many components is part of the same Reynolds projection.

<a id="meas:cyclic-trace"></a>
<a id="meas:parameter-map"></a>
The Hecke insertion contributes a finite-dimensional cyclic trace. For linear maps around $d$ copies of $D$ one has $$\label{meas:cyclic-trace}
 \operatorname{tr}\bigl(\operatorname{cyc}\circ
                      (A_1\otimes\cdots\otimes A_d);D^{\otimes d}\bigr)
 =\operatorname{tr}(A_d\cdots A_1;D).$$ Expanding in a basis proves the identity: the cyclic identification of indices leaves precisely the matrix entries of the indicated product. The framed evaluation description in Theorem <a href="#mod:model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mod:model">[mod:model]</a> applies this identity to the geometric points above $v$. The triple translations contribute $\lambda_e(q^{d/2})$, and the residual semisimple product is compact and centralizes the triple. Unipotent factors may be discarded in this trace. We obtain a continuous map $$\label{meas:parameter-map}
 \beta_e:L_{e,c}\longrightarrow B_{\widehat G.e}.$$ Any lifted-degree sign on the insertion contributes $\lambda_e(-1)$ to the compact part. This is already included in $B_{\widehat G.e}$. Contravariant character conventions give the inverse parameter, which lies in the same compact set by Lemma <a href="#desc:compactsets" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="desc:compactsets">31</a>.

<a id="meas:orbit-measure"></a>
Remove the insertion and write $a_{M,N,e}(k)$ for the alternating character density of the supported contribution. The lifted-degree sign may be used inside <a href="#meas:compact-projection" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="meas:compact-projection">[meas:compact-projection]</a>: after taking invariants it is the true cohomological sign, as established in Proposition <a href="#orb:hom-calculation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="orb:hom-calculation">19</a>. The density is continuous and absolutely integrable by Lemma <a href="#meas:supported-convergence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="meas:supported-convergence">26</a>. The contribution of $O$ is therefore the finite complex measure $$\label{meas:orbit-measure}
 (\beta_e)_*\bigl(a_{M,N,e}(k)\,dk\bigr).$$ Its total variation is bounded by $\int|a_{M,N,e}|\,dk$. Formula <a href="#meas:cyclic-trace" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="meas:cyclic-trace">[meas:cyclic-trace]</a> shows that its $\chi_D$-moment is exactly the orbit contribution with the Hecke insertion.

<a id="stmt-meas-common-support"></a>
<div id="meas:common-support" class="proposition">

**Proposition 27**. *Suppose $M,N$ belong to one retained component and have closed $z$-support bounds $Z_M,Z_N\subset\mathfrak j$. Their measure satisfies <a id="meas:common-support-bound"></a>$$\label{meas:common-support-bound}
 \operatorname{supp}(\nu_{f_N,g_M})
 \subset
 \bigcup_{e\in Z_M\cap Z_N}B_{\widehat G.e}.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-meas-common-support"><summary>Proof of stmt-meas-common-support</summary>

*Proof.* Sum <a href="#meas:orbit-measure" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="meas:orbit-measure">[meas:orbit-measure]</a> over the finitely many stable nilpotent orbits in the common support. Lemma <a href="#meas:supported-convergence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="meas:supported-convergence">26</a> gives additivity, and Lemma <a href="#meas:hom-functions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="meas:hom-functions">25</a> identifies all its moments. Orbits outside the common support give zero differential Hom; permuted orbits give zero trace. The support bound follows from <a href="#meas:parameter-map" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="meas:parameter-map">[meas:parameter-map]</a>. Uniqueness follows from the density of characters in $C(B)$ in Lemma <a href="#desc:compactsets" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="desc:compactsets">31</a>. ◻

</details>

By Theorem <a href="#tr:spanning" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="tr:spanning">23</a>, every pair of functions is a finite linear combination of the pairs just considered. Each vector uses only finitely many retained fixed components, because the categorical trace is the direct sum on such components. Cross-component Homs vanish. Adding the finite measures above therefore proves existence in Theorem <a href="#meas:detector" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="meas:detector">24</a> for every $f,g$, and character density proves uniqueness independent of the chosen expansion. The construction is bilinear. Proposition <a href="#meas:common-support" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="meas:common-support">27</a> already proves the forward implication in <a href="#meas:criterion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="meas:criterion">[meas:criterion]</a>.

<a id="pm-h-28"></a>
#### Nondegeneracy of the top-orbit contribution

The decisive test concerns total mass. Canceling a common nonvanishing density will isolate a simple coefficient, so injectivity of the parameter map $\beta_e$ is unnecessary.

We must also show that a nonzero quotient class cannot be hidden by cancellation of its measure. Fix a stable orbit $O=J.e$. Let $P,Q$ be finite-dimensional equivariant $D_e$-modules, with compatible cyclic structures, and take the compact orbit-closure lifts of Proposition <a href="#orb:weil-lifts" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="orb:weil-lifts">18</a>. We put the test coefficient $Q$ first and the input coefficient $P$ second in a Hom.

<a id="meas:top-density"></a>
<a id="meas:universal-density-factors"></a>
On an open subset where $O$ is closed and the proper boundary of its closure has been removed, both lifts are already supported on $O$. Consequently local cohomology with that support is the actual Hom complex. There is no additional series of inverse normal powers to put into its Euler character. Proposition <a href="#orb:hom-calculation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="orb:hom-calculation">19</a> gives $$\label{meas:top-density}
 a_{Q,P,e}(k)=p_e(k)\,
     \operatorname{tr}\bigl(k\tau_e;\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{D_e}(Q,P)\bigr),
 \qquad k\in L_{e,c}.$$ The action on the Hom uses the inverse structure on the test as in <a href="#meas:hom-action" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="meas:hom-action">[meas:hom-action]</a>. The factor $p_e$ is the graded Euler character of $$\label{meas:universal-density-factors}
 \mathbb C[\ker e]\otimes
 \Lambda^\bullet(N_e[-1])\otimes
 \Lambda^\bullet(\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(U_e)^*[-1]).$$ The identification of constant Clifford super-intertwiners with ordinary intertwiners, and its compatibility with lifted parity and tensor twists, are part of Proposition <a href="#orb:hom-calculation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="orb:hom-calculation">19</a>.

The invariant-base Koszul complexes used to obtain actual compact lifts do not alter <a href="#meas:top-density" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="meas:top-density">[meas:top-density]</a> except by constant nonzero factors. Indeed, expand their finite exterior terms by additivity in each input. Their equation spaces are $J$-invariant, so these factors are independent of $k$; their Frobenius eigenvalues and inverse eigenvalues are never one. The exterior traces are therefore nonzero, as in Lemma <a href="#tr:invariant-support" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="tr:invariant-support">20</a>. We rescale the trace classes by these constants. This permits computation first on the uncompleted orbit model while retaining actual compact tests throughout.

<a id="stmt-meas-cancel-density"></a>
<div id="meas:cancel-density" class="lemma">

**Lemma 28** (Cancellation of the universal density). *The function $p_e$ is continuous and nowhere zero on $L_{e,c}$. Its reciprocal is a uniform limit of finite complex linear combinations of twisted characters of finite-dimensional $L_e$-representations with compatible transporter structures. Such a twisted character can be inserted in <a href="#meas:top-density" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="meas:top-density">[meas:top-density]</a> by tensoring the test coefficient with the dual representation, in lifted degree zero.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-meas-cancel-density"><summary>Proof of stmt-meas-cancel-density</summary>

<a id="meas:determinant-factor"></a>
*Proof.* Decompose each of the finite-dimensional spaces in <a href="#meas:universal-density-factors" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="meas:universal-density-factors">[meas:universal-density-factors]</a> by lifted degree. The symmetric algebra contributes inverse determinants, and each exterior algebra contributes determinants. Thus $p_e$ is a finite product of terms $$\label{meas:determinant-factor}
 \det(1-\varepsilon kA;W)^{\eta},
 \qquad \varepsilon\in\{1,-1\},\quad \eta\in\{1,-1\},$$ where $W$ is a finite-dimensional $L_e$-representation with transporter $A$, and all eigenvalues of $kA$ have modulus strictly less than one, uniformly in $k$. The sign $\varepsilon$ incorporates the specified lifted-degree shift. The strict inequality follows from the weight bounds: all factors outside the constant coefficient Hom have negative absolute weight. Each determinant in <a href="#meas:determinant-factor" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="meas:determinant-factor">[meas:determinant-factor]</a> is therefore nonzero, proving the first assertion.

The identities $$\det(1-A;W)=\sum_{j=0}^{\dim W}(-1)^j
                         \operatorname{tr}(A;\Lambda^jW),
 \qquad
 \det(1-A;W)^{-1}=\sum_{n\geq0}
                         \operatorname{tr}(A;\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^nW)$$ apply also with the sign $\varepsilon$. The second series converges uniformly here: its $n$th term is bounded by $\dim(\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^nW)\rho^n$ for a fixed $\rho<1$. Finite products of these identities express $1/p_e$ as the required uniform limit. Every finite term is the twisted character of a finite tensor product of symmetric and exterior powers of the allowed representation data; scalar factors may be put into its coefficient or cyclic structure.

Finally, $$\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{D_e}(Q\otimes R^*,P)
 \simeq R\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{D_e}(Q,P)$$ with the induced cyclic actions. Under the parity identification already used for <a href="#meas:top-density" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="meas:top-density">[meas:top-density]</a>, its character multiplies that density by the twisted character of $R$. The extension construction in Proposition <a href="#orb:weil-lifts" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="orb:weil-lifts">18</a> applies to $Q\otimes R^*$ as well. Each finite approximant is consequently realized by a finite linear combination of permitted compact test classes. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-meas-top-detection"></a>
<div id="meas:top-detection" class="lemma">

**Lemma 29** (Top quotient detection). *Every nonzero finite linear combination of fixed-simple classes in the $O$-quotient is detected by a compact test supported on $\overline O$: its top-orbit measure has nonzero total mass on $B_{\widehat G.e}$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-meas-top-detection"><summary>Proof of stmt-meas-top-detection</summary>

<a id="meas:unweighted-coefficient-pairing"></a>
*Proof.* Write the quotient class as $u=\sum_P c_P[P]$, with finitely many nonzero coefficients. Suppose its top mass were zero against every permitted test lift. Fix a test coefficient $Q$. By tensoring $Q$ with the duals of the representations in Lemma <a href="#meas:cancel-density" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="meas:cancel-density">28</a>, the assumption gives $$\int_{L_{e,c}}p_e(k)h(k)
    \sum_P c_P\operatorname{tr}
          \bigl(k\tau_e;\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{D_e}(Q,P)\bigr)\,dk=0$$ for every finite character combination $h$ used there. Letting these $h$ converge uniformly to $1/p_e$ gives $$\label{meas:unweighted-coefficient-pairing}
 0=\sum_P c_P\operatorname{tr}
            \bigl(\tau_e;\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{D_e,L_e}(Q,P)\bigr).$$ This is legitimate integration against a fixed continuous function, not a limit of automorphic functions.

The equivariant Clifford coefficient category is semisimple. Choose $Q$ to be any fixed simple appearing in $u$, with the matching structure. For $P\not\simeq Q$ its Hom is zero; for $P\simeq Q$ it is the scalar endomorphism line and the action is the identity. Thus <a href="#meas:unweighted-coefficient-pairing" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="meas:unweighted-coefficient-pairing">[meas:unweighted-coefficient-pairing]</a> says $c_Q=0$. Repeating this contradicts $u\ne0$. Some actual finite test must therefore have nonzero top mass. The argument requires no injectivity of $\beta_e:L_{e,c}\to B_{\widehat G.e}$: the total mass alone suffices. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-29"></a>
#### Excluding boundary cancellation

<a id="stmt-meas-boundary-rank"></a>
<div id="meas:boundary-rank" class="lemma">

**Lemma 30**. *If $e'\in\overline{J.e}\setminus J.e$, then $\dim(\widehat G.e')<\dim(\widehat G.e)$. In particular the two ambient nilpotent orbits, and their compact sets, are distinct.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-meas-boundary-rank"><summary>Proof of stmt-meas-boundary-rank</summary>

*Proof.* Since $J$ is reductive in characteristic zero, choose a $J$-stable complement $W$ in $\widehat{\mathfrak g}=\mathfrak j\oplus W$. The adjoint rank on $\mathfrak j$ is the dimension of the $J$-orbit and strictly drops on a proper boundary orbit. The rank on $W$ cannot increase under specialization, because the condition that a matrix have rank at most a given integer is closed. Hence $$\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}(\operatorname{ad}(e')|\widehat{\mathfrak g})
 <\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}(\operatorname{ad}(e)|\widehat{\mathfrak g}).$$ These ranks are the ambient orbit dimensions. The same argument works for a disconnected $J$, whose orbits are finite unions of orbits of its identity component. Disjointness of the compact sets follows from Lemma <a href="#desc:compactsets" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="desc:compactsets">31</a>. ◻

</details>

We finish the converse in Theorem <a href="#meas:detector" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="meas:detector">24</a>. Suppose $f\notin\mathcal F_Y\mathcal A_{\mathbb C}$. By Theorem <a href="#tr:spanning" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="tr:spanning">23</a>, choose a retained fixed component on which its trace class does not lie in the supported trace for $Y\cap\mathfrak j$. Starting with this closed subset, filter the nilpotent cone in $\mathfrak j$ by closed invariant subsets, adding one Frobenius orbit of $J$-orbits at a time, compatibly with closure. Write $T_0\subset T_1\subset\cdots\subset T_m$ for the resulting supported trace subspaces, with $T_0$ the trace for $Y\cap\mathfrak j$. Let $i$ be the least index for which the component class lies in $T_i$. Then $i>0$, and its image $u\in T_i/T_{i-1}$ is nonzero. A nontrivially permuted stratum has zero trace, so this step adds a single Frobenius-stable orbit $O=J.e$ outside $Y\cap\mathfrak j$.

Express $u$ as a finite combination of the fixed-simple classes in the $O$-quotient, and choose their compact orbit-closure lifts as in the proof of Theorem <a href="#tr:spanning" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="tr:spanning">23</a>. Subtract their sum from the component class. The remainder belongs to $T_{i-1}$ and, by the same theorem, is represented by a finite combination of compact Weil classes with that earlier support. Apply Lemma <a href="#meas:top-detection" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="meas:top-detection">29</a> to $u$ and these lifts to obtain a compact test supported on $\overline O$ with nonzero top mass. The earlier support bound defining $T_{i-1}$ meets $\overline O$ only in $\overline O\setminus O$. Proposition <a href="#meas:common-support" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="meas:common-support">27</a> and Lemma <a href="#meas:boundary-rank" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="meas:boundary-rank">30</a> therefore show that the remainder, and the proper-boundary contributions of the chosen lifts, have zero mass on $B_{\widehat G.e}$. This also excludes contributions from incomparable $J$-orbits in the same ambient nilpotent orbit. Other components give zero Hom. Therefore $$\nu_{f,g}(B_{\widehat G.e})\ne0$$ for the resulting genuine finitely supported function test $g$. Since $e\notin Y$, the invariant set $Y$ does not contain its ambient orbit, and $B_{\widehat G.e}$ is disjoint from $B_Y$. The asserted support condition fails. This proves the converse and completes the proof of Theorem <a href="#meas:detector" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="meas:detector">24</a>.

No step used a rational point of $X$, a cuspidality condition, or Hecke-finiteness. Empty and full $Y$ are included, and the closed point $v$ may have any positive degree.

<a id="desc:section"></a>
### Compact spectral sets and rational descent

We first verify the elementary properties of the sets in <a href="#intro:compactsets" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="intro:compactsets">[intro:compactsets]</a>. These properties supply the uniqueness used in Theorem <a href="#meas:detector" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="meas:detector">24</a>; their proof is independent of that theorem.

<a id="stmt-desc-compactsets"></a>
<div id="desc:compactsets" class="lemma">

**Lemma 31**. *There are finitely many sets $B_o$. Each is compact and independent of the triple and compact form used to define it, and distinct orbits give disjoint sets. The algebra of representation characters restricted to $B=\bigcup_oB_o$ contains the constants, separates points and is closed under complex conjugation. It is uniformly dense in $C(B,\mathbb C)$. Each $B_o$ is preserved by inversion and by multiplication by a finite-order central element of $\widehat G_\mathbb C$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-desc-compactsets"><summary>Proof of stmt-desc-compactsets</summary>

*Proof.* Nilpotent orbits in a complex semisimple Lie algebra are finite in number. Triples for a fixed orbit are conjugate, and maximal compact forms of a complex reductive group, including its components, are conjugate. Consequently their images in the affine conjugacy quotient give the same $B_o$. The image of a compact group under the displayed continuous map is compact. The finite union $B$, as a compact subspace of a complex affine variety, is metrizable.

Choose a maximal torus $T\subset\widehat G_\mathbb C$. Polar decomposition in $T(\mathbb C)$ gives $$T(\mathbb C)=T(\mathbb R_{>0})\times T_{\mathrm{cpt}},$$ where the positive factor is intrinsically described by the absolute values of all characters. This decomposition is Weyl-equivariant. The centralizer of the torus $\lambda_e(\mathbb G_m)$ in $\widehat G_\mathbb C$ is a connected reductive Levi subgroup. It contains $s$, and a maximal torus of this Levi containing the semisimple element $s$ also contains its central torus $\lambda_e(\mathbb G_m)$. Thus $\lambda_e$ and $s$ can be placed in a common maximal torus. The positive part of $\lambda_e(\sqrt r)s$ is exactly $\lambda_e(\sqrt r)$. Its Weyl orbit determines the dominant cocharacter $\lambda_e$: evaluation of cocharacters at $\sqrt r>1$ is injective.

For completeness, that cocharacter determines the nilpotent orbit without forgetting any distinction in type D. Fix $\lambda$ and write $\widehat{\mathfrak g}_i$ for its weight spaces. Whenever a triple has diagonal cocharacter $\lambda$, the $\mathfrak{sl}_2$ decomposition gives $$[\widehat{\mathfrak g}_0,e]=\widehat{\mathfrak g}_2.$$ Indeed, the raising map from weight zero to weight two is surjective on each irreducible $\mathfrak{sl}_2$ summand. Thus the centralizer of $\lambda$ has an open orbit through $e$ in the irreducible vector space $\widehat{\mathfrak g}_2$. Two such open orbits must coincide. It follows that two parameters with the same positive part have the same ambient nilpotent orbit. This proves disjointness; it is also the description underlying <a id="pm-cite-71"></a>[[GLR](#ref-GLR), Lemma 3.1.3].

Representation characters separate semisimple conjugacy values in a connected complex reductive group: restriction to $T$ identifies their complex span with $\mathbb C[T]^W$ <a id="pm-cite-72"></a>[[MilneAG](#ref-MilneAG), Theorem 22.38]. Hence their restrictions separate points of $B$. To check complex conjugation, write $a=\lambda_e(\sqrt r)$. For a representation $D$, $D(a)$ is diagonalizable with positive real eigenvalues and commutes with $D(s)$; on each of its eigenspaces $D(s)$ is unitary after choosing an invariant Hermitian form for the compact centralizer. Therefore $$\overline{\chi_D(as)}=\chi_D(as^{-1}).$$ A Weyl element in the triple’s $\mathrm{SL}_2$ conjugates $a$ to $a^{-1}$ and commutes with $s$. Thus $$\chi_D(as^{-1})=\chi_D(a^{-1}s^{-1})=\chi_{D^\vee}(as).$$ The same representation $D^\vee$ works simultaneously on every $B_o$. The character algebra is consequently self-adjoint, and the complex Stone–Weierstrass Theorem proves its density on the finite compact union.

The triple Weyl element also shows that $(as)^{-1}$ is conjugate to $as^{-1}$, proving invariance under inversion. A finite-order central element lies in the chosen compact triple centralizer: adjoining it gives a compact group, and maximality leaves that group unchanged. Multiplication by it therefore preserves $B_o$. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-desc-unique-measure"></a>
<div id="desc:unique-measure" class="corollary">

**Corollary 32**. *A finite complex Borel measure on $B$ is determined by its integrals against all representation characters.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-desc-unique-measure"><summary>Proof of stmt-desc-unique-measure</summary>

*Proof.* Integration against a finite complex measure is a continuous functional on $C(B,\mathbb C)$ for the uniform norm. Lemma <a href="#desc:compactsets" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="desc:compactsets">31</a> makes the character algebra dense. Equality on the characters therefore gives equality of the continuous functionals and hence of the measures. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-31"></a>
#### Independence of the coefficient isomorphism

All algebraically closed characteristic-zero coefficient fields below are considered as abstract fields. Both $E$ and $\mathbb C$ have transcendence degree $2^{\aleph_0}$ over $\mathbb Q$, so there are field isomorphisms $E\simeq\mathbb C$. No continuity or compatibility with a selected embedding of the algebraic numbers is used.

<a id="stmt-desc-coefficient-independence"></a>
<div id="desc:coefficient-independence" class="proposition">

**Proposition 33**. *For a fixed closed invariant support $Y$, the subspace $\iota_*(\mathcal F_Y\mathcal A_E)\subset\mathcal A_\mathbb C$ is independent of the abstract isomorphism $\iota:E\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb C$, after matching nilpotent orbits by the split root datum. Consequently $\mathcal F_Y\mathcal A_E$ is invariant under every automorphism of $E$ fixing $\mathbb Q$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-desc-coefficient-independence"><summary>Proof of stmt-desc-coefficient-independence</summary>

*Proof.* The point functions identify every transported ambient space with the same $\mathbb C^{(S)}$. The pinned split form identifies representation characters by highest weight across coefficient fields. The pairing <a href="#intro:pairing" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="intro:pairing">[intro:pairing]</a> has rational coefficients. Spherical Hecke correspondences count the same finite-field modifications under each transport. Their normalization may be chosen on the complex side using the fixed positive $\sqrt q$, so their operators $T_D$ are the same for each $\iota$.

If a transported choice of $\sqrt q$ differs from this one, set $$z=(2\rho_G)(-1)\in Z(\widehat G)[2],$$ where $2\rho_G$ is the sum of the positive roots of $G$, viewed as a cocharacter of its dual group. The Satake parity convention at the degree-$d$ point introduces $z^d$: a highest weight $\mu$ acquires the sign $(-1)^{d\langle 2\rho_G,\mu\rangle}$, which is exactly its central character at $z^d$. This convention is also encoded by the modified dual group in <a id="pm-cite-73"></a>[[RicharzScholbach](#ref-RicharzScholbach), Remark 6.7 and Section 6.4]. On characters the change is therefore translation by $z^d$, and on moment measures it is the corresponding pushforward. By Lemma <a href="#desc:compactsets" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="desc:compactsets">31</a>, this translation preserves each $B_o$. The contragredient convention similarly preserves each $B_o$ by inversion. Thus these conventions do not change the support test.

Nilpotent orbits of the split group are identified across these fields using the fixed pinned $\mathbb Q$-form and their triple cocharacters, or equivalently their weighted Dynkin diagrams. No outer automorphism is introduced in this identification. In particular the orbit labels in $Y$ and the compact set $B_Y$ are fixed independently of $\iota$. Theorem <a href="#meas:detector" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="meas:detector">24</a> and Corollary <a href="#desc:unique-measure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="desc:unique-measure">32</a> now characterize the transported subspace by the same moment equations and the same support condition for every test $g\in\mathcal A_\mathbb C$. The subspaces are equal.

Fix one $\iota$ and let $\tau\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(E/\mathbb Q)$. Comparing $\iota$ with $\iota\circ\tau$ gives $\iota_*(\mathcal F_Y\mathcal A_E)=\iota_*\tau_*(\mathcal F_Y\mathcal A_E)$. Applying $\iota^{-1}$ proves invariance. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-32"></a>
#### Descent on finite coordinate sets

<a id="stmt-desc-descent"></a>
<div id="desc:descent" class="lemma">

**Lemma 34**. *Let $E$ be an algebraically closed extension of $\mathbb Q$, and let $V_\mathbb Q$ have a specified basis, possibly infinite. If $W\subset E\otimes_\mathbb QV_\mathbb Q$ is invariant under every $\tau\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(E/\mathbb Q)$, then $$E\otimes_\mathbb Q(W\cap V_\mathbb Q)\longrightarrow W$$ is an isomorphism.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-desc-descent"><summary>Proof of stmt-desc-descent</summary>

*Proof.* The fixed field of $\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(E/\mathbb Q)$ is $\mathbb Q$. Indeed, an algebraic element outside $\mathbb Q$ has a different conjugate, and the corresponding embedding of its number field extends to an automorphism of $E$. A transcendental element can be included in a transcendence basis; the substitution $t\mapsto t+1$ on that basis element extends from the rational function field to its algebraic closure $E$. Thus it too is moved by an automorphism over $\mathbb Q$.

Let $I$ be a finite subset of the specified basis, choose an order on $I$, and put $$W_I=W\cap E^I.$$ This is an invariant finite-dimensional subspace. Its unique reduced row-echelon basis matrix is unchanged by every automorphism over $\mathbb Q$: field automorphisms preserve the row space, the pivot positions and the normalization conditions. Every matrix entry therefore belongs to the fixed field $\mathbb Q$. Its nonzero rows give a rational basis, and hence $$W_I=E\otimes_\mathbb Q(W_I\cap\mathbb Q^I).$$ Every vector of $E\otimes_\mathbb QV_\mathbb Q$ has finite support in the specified basis. Taking the directed union over $I$ gives the claimed surjectivity. Injectivity follows by tensoring the inclusion $W\cap V_\mathbb Q\hookrightarrow V_\mathbb Q$ with the flat $\mathbb Q$-module $E$. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-desc-descent-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-desc-descent</summary>

*Proof of Theorem <a href="#intro:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="intro:main">2</a>.* Proposition <a href="#desc:coefficient-independence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="desc:coefficient-independence">33</a> makes $W=\mathcal F_Y\mathcal A_E$ invariant under all coefficient automorphisms over $\mathbb Q$. Apply Lemma <a href="#desc:descent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="desc:descent">34</a> to $V_\mathbb Q=\mathcal A_\mathbb Q$ with its point-function basis. This is exactly the map <a href="#intro:main-map" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="intro:main-map">[intro:main-map]</a>.

The argument applies to every closed invariant $Y$, including the empty set and the full nilpotent cone. The trace calculation uses all retained fixed components and actual finite linear combinations of Weil trace classes. The detector allows every finitely supported test function. Consequently the result holds for the entire specified space, without any additional finiteness or cuspidality assumption. ◻

</details>

The argument also compares coefficient primes on the full function space under the same four conditions of Assumption <a href="#intro:characteristic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="intro:characteristic">1</a>. Put $p=\operatorname{char}(\mathbb F_q)$ and $E_\ell=\overline{\mathbb Q}_\ell$ for $\ell\ne p$. Let $Y_\ell\subset\widehat{\mathcal N}_{E_\ell}$ be closed invariant supports with the same nilpotent-orbit labels under the pinned split dual group. Then, as subspaces of $\mathcal A_\mathbb Q$, $$\mathcal A_\mathbb Q\cap\mathcal F_{Y_\ell}\mathcal A_{E_\ell}
 =\mathcal A_\mathbb Q\cap\mathcal F_{Y_{\ell'}}\mathcal A_{E_{\ell'}}
 \qquad(\ell,\ell'\ne p).$$ Indeed, choose the same closed point $v$ and abstract isomorphisms $E_\ell\simeq\mathbb C$. With the positive square-root Satake normalization, the moment equations for every $f,g\in\mathcal A_\mathbb C$ and the compact support sets in Theorem <a href="#meas:detector" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="meas:detector">24</a> are the same for every prime, as in the proof of Proposition <a href="#desc:coefficient-independence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="desc:coefficient-independence">33</a>. Uniqueness of the measures gives the same transported filtration in $\mathcal A_\mathbb C$. Intersecting with $\mathcal A_\mathbb Q$, which every coefficient isomorphism fixes, proves the equality. As the matched closed orbit subset varies, these common rational subspaces form a filtration of $\mathcal A_\mathbb Q$ whose $E_\ell$ scalar extension is the original Arthur filtration, by Theorem <a href="#intro:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="intro:main">2</a>. This compares rational function subspaces; it does not identify spectral support categories or individual eigenobjects.

<a id="pm-h-33"></a>
#### Unramified cuspidal comparison

<a id="stmt-desc-cuspidal-comparison"></a>
<div id="desc:cuspidal-comparison" class="corollary">

<a id="desc:cuspidal-equalities"></a>
**Corollary 35** (Cuspidal comparison across coefficient primes). *Keep the curve $X/\mathbb F_q$, the split connected semisimple group $G/\mathbb F_q$, and all four conditions of Assumption <a href="#intro:characteristic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="intro:characteristic">1</a>. Put $p=\operatorname{char}(\mathbb F_q)$ and $E_\ell=\overline{\mathbb Q}_\ell$ for $\ell\ne p$. At unramified level $D=0$, let $C_{0,\mathbb Q}\subset\mathcal A_\mathbb Q$ be the cuspidal function space and $C_{0,\mathcal O,\mathbb Q}$ its rational orbit summands from <a id="pm-cite-74"></a>[[CuspidalArthur](#ref-CuspidalArthur), Theorem 1.1], and set $C_{0,\ell}=E_\ell\otimes_\mathbb QC_{0,\mathbb Q}$. For each $\ell\ne p$, let $Y_\ell\subset\widehat{\mathcal N}_{E_\ell}$ be closed and invariant, with the same nilpotent-orbit subset $\mathscr Y$ under the fixed pinned split rational dual group. Write $Y=(Y_\ell)_{\ell\ne p}$ and define $$F^{\mathbb Q}_{Y,\ell}:=\mathcal A_\mathbb Q\cap\mathcal F_{Y_\ell}\mathcal A_{E_\ell},
 \qquad
 V_{Y,\mathbb Q}:=\bigoplus_{\mathcal O\in\mathscr Y}C_{0,\mathcal O,\mathbb Q}.$$ Then $$\label{desc:cuspidal-equalities}
 \begin{split}
 C_{0,\mathbb Q}\cap F^{\mathbb Q}_{Y,\ell}&=V_{Y,\mathbb Q},\\
 C_{0,\ell}\cap\mathcal F_{Y_\ell}\mathcal A_{E_\ell}
   &=E_\ell\otimes_\mathbb QV_{Y,\mathbb Q}.
 \end{split}$$ The intersections are taken in the corresponding finitely supported function spaces. The equalities include the zero cusp space and empty or full $Y_\ell$.*

</div>

This is precisely the unramified cuspidal common-scope form of <a id="pm-cite-75"></a>[[GLR](#ref-GLR), Conjectures 3.4.9 and 3.4.11]. The additional identification with the companion’s orbit summands concerns the $D=0$ cuspidal subspace.

<details id="proof-stmt-desc-cuspidal-comparison"><summary>Proof of stmt-desc-cuspidal-comparison</summary>

*Proof.* For split $G$, the companion’s unramified double quotient is the set $S$ of bundle classes <a id="pm-cite-76"></a>[[CuspidalArthur](#ref-CuspidalArthur), Section 6.1], giving the stated point-function embeddings. Fix $\ell$, an abstract isomorphism $\iota:E_\ell\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb C$, and a closed point $v$ with $r=q^{\deg v}$. The finite reduced global Hecke image on the cusp space gives finitely many joint eigenspaces <a id="pm-cite-77"></a>[[CuspidalArthur](#ref-CuspidalArthur), Lemma 6.1]. Restrict to the spherical algebra at $v$ and group all joint characters with the same one-place Satake class. The ordinary Hecke compatibility of Theorem <a href="#inp:trace" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="inp:trace">5</a> and the companion’s volume-one normalization give $$C_{0,\mathbb C}:=\mathbb C\otimes_\mathbb QC_{0,\mathbb Q}
   =\bigoplus_{t\in\Sigma_v}C_t,
 \qquad T_V|_{C_t}=\chi_V(t),$$ where the $t$ are distinct classes in the positive $\sqrt r$ convention and $V$ is any finite-dimensional representation of $\widehat G_\mathbb C$. The possible square-root correction is a finite central translate <a id="pm-cite-78"></a>[[CuspidalArthur](#ref-CuspidalArthur), Remark 6.3], and a contragredient convention gives inversion; both preserve every $B_{\mathcal O}$ by Lemma <a href="#desc:compactsets" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="desc:compactsets">31</a>.

The companion’s orbit descent assigns each joint character one orbit label, independent of the good place and complex embedding, and the complex scalar extension of each rational orbit summand is the sum of the joint eigenspaces with that label <a id="pm-cite-79"></a>[[CuspidalArthur](#ref-CuspidalArthur), Lemma 6.2 and Propositions 6.5–6.6]. Every occurring embedded class of label $\mathcal O$ lies in $B_{\mathcal O}$ by the all-embeddings compactness defining $R_{r,\mathcal O}$. Since the $B_{\mathcal O}$ are disjoint, all joint eigenspaces grouped into one $C_t$ have the same label. Thus $\Sigma_v\subset B$ and $$\mathbb C\otimes_\mathbb QC_{0,\mathcal O,\mathbb Q}
   =\bigoplus_{t\in\Sigma_v\cap B_{\mathcal O}}C_t.$$ For the common support put $B_Y=\bigcup_{\mathcal O\in\mathscr Y}B_{\mathcal O}$.

Write $f=\sum_t f_t\in C_{0,\mathbb C}$. For every $g\in\mathcal A_\mathbb C$, the finite atomic measure $\mu_{f,g}:=\sum_t[f_t,g]\delta_t$ on $B$ satisfies $$[T_Vf,g]=\sum_t\chi_V(t)[f_t,g]
           =\int_B\chi_V\,d\mu_{f,g}.$$ Corollary <a href="#desc:unique-measure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="desc:unique-measure">32</a> identifies $\mu_{f,g}$ with the detector measure $\nu_{f,g}$. If $f_t\ne0$, choose $b\in S$ with $f_t(b)\ne0$ and take the point function $g=\delta_b$. Then $$\mu_{f,\delta_b}(\{t\})=[f_t,\delta_b]
             =\frac{f_t(b)}{|\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(b)|}\ne0.$$ The atoms are distinct, so no other local component cancels this mass. The test need not be cuspidal. Consequently all detector measures are supported in $B_Y$ exactly when $f_t=0$ for every $t\notin B_Y$. Theorem <a href="#meas:detector" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="meas:detector">24</a> and the preceding orbit decomposition give $$C_{0,\mathbb C}\cap\iota_*(\mathcal F_{Y_\ell}\mathcal A_{E_\ell})
   =\mathbb C\otimes_\mathbb QV_{Y,\mathbb Q}.$$ Pulling back by $\iota$, which fixes $\mathbb Q$, proves the second equality in <a href="#desc:cuspidal-equalities" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="desc:cuspidal-equalities">[desc:cuspidal-equalities]</a>. Intersect it with $C_{0,\mathbb Q}$ and use $C_{0,\mathbb Q}\cap(E_\ell\otimes_\mathbb QV_{Y,\mathbb Q})=V_{Y,\mathbb Q}$ to obtain the first. ◻

</details>
